# Journal

What we've actually shipped. Reverse-chronological, one section per working day. Bold IDs (e.g. **[D10](TODO.md#d--bots-live-trading)**) cross-reference [TODO.md](TODO.md).

> **Maintenance rule (Claude):** append an entry at the end of any session that produces durable work — TODO closures, features, bug fixes, discoveries. Skip routine commits (typo fixes, reformatting). Keep bullets short; link to the commit or doc if more context is worth a click. Don't re-read every TODO to write an entry — just log what happened in the session.

## 2026-06-10

- **[F407](TODO-archive.md#f407) news-panel alias quality v2 + full re-run — +557 tickers and +1.18M rows recovered, $0.** Ground-truthed GDELT's actual org-name forms with three cheap one-day queries before designing: GDELT strips '&' ('procter gamble'), converts hyphens to spaces ('coca cola'), drops possessives ('mcdonald' 1,782/day vs 'mcdonalds' 292), fuses 'JPMorgan', and colloquial names crush legal ones ('facebook' 9,157/day vs 'meta platforms inc' 61; 'google' 6,195 vs 'alphabet inc' 59). Normalization v2 mirrored in both python and BigQuery SQL; manifest cleaning strips '(The)' parentheticals and leading 'The' (the Boeing/Goldman killers); every progressive suffix-strip stage is its own core alias; new curated 'alt' channel (facebook→META, google→GOOGL, disney→DIS, jpmorgan forms→JPM) separately excludable for precision work. Panel v2: **6.37M rows × 3,798 tickers**, probe 5/5 with pre-stated recovery anchors (BA 0→3.45M lifetime articles, JPM alt 789k). Review-wave adversarially CONFIRMED python/SQL parity at the byte level (0 defects, 1 cosmetic P3); its suggested alt-collision build assertion applied. AT&T verified absent from GKG V2Organizations in every form — documented unfixable upstream gap. Quota: ~700GB of the 1TB June free tier consumed; F408 (incremental month-append) keeps future updates at ~3GB.

## 2026-06-09

- **[F399](TODO-archive.md#f399)–F403 Desk Discovery Mode, Phase 0 — free-data foundation modules built + F338-verified.** New idea from John: a second Desk mode that *scans data for what leads the forward return* instead of John writing a premise — a premise **generator** feeding the existing F397 premise **tester**. Brainstormed the whole design (companion note `docs/plans/2026-06-09-phase1-discovery-scan-companion-note.md`): hunt lead-lag predictors (X,k) not anything-vs-anything; two-window discovery/confirm + **deflated-Sharpe attempt tax** (Bailey–López de Prado) so breadth can't dredge OOS; composite screen (IC + rank IC + hit-rate + sub-period stability, pre-stated weights); auto-mint survivors as premise cards; liquidity-as-a-knob; **benchmark relative** (market drift + sector co-movement + the F369/F371 +1.5pp baseline → dose-response headline, never mean-vs-zero, John 2026-06-09). That scan needs a wider data panel, so Phase 0 builds it (spec `docs/plans/2026-06-09-phase0-free-data-foundation-spec.md`). **Sharadar rejected** (the only survivorship-free price source) — its license requires deleting the data *and anything derived from it* within 30 days of cancel, incompatible with a reproducible-research program; survivorship stays a stamped permanent caveat on free (yfinance) data. Built four standalone ingest modules via orchestrator cycle (4 parallel sonnet implementers → inline F338 real-data gate → Tier C review-wave → 1 holistic fixer): **F400** widen universe (NASDAQ symbol dir → ~12,384 tradable names; UNIVERSE_V2 floor + filer-flag become per-date *labels*/knobs, not membership gates), **F401** analyst up/downgrades (yfinance rating actions), **F402** GDELT news volume+tone, **F403** FINRA short interest. Each F338-probed on real data with pre-stated anchors (AAPL split-adjusted + Lehman correctly absent; Goldman's real AAPL downgrade on the exact date; SVB collapse = 34× news spike, tone −2.87; GME short interest 61,782,730 shares reproduced to the share). Review-wave (correctness/python/data-integrity/performance + adversarial verify) caught **8 confirmed findings incl. 2 P0s the probes structurally couldn't see**: C-001 `build_daily_panel` duplicate-column crash on any multi-date ticker (the whole 10y run was a dead letter — fixed + A6 regression anchor added), and PERF-01 GDELT-at-12k-tickers = 34–550h infeasible (de-scoped to the liquid universe; full-universe news → **F405** GKG-bulk migration). Orchestrator-caught (reading artifacts, not digests): `is_sec_filer` was a 2012–2021-window artifact mislabeled (→ renamed `in_liquid_universe_v1`), and the "exchange-listed short interest starts June-2021" claim was false — empirically verified GE/BAC/F/GME present in 2019 files, so the panel goes back to **2018** (~6yr not 3, more Phase-1 power). All 7 fixes applied + verified (short-int probe 6/6, ratings 5/5 byte-identical after vectorizing). **Modules built + verified; full-scale fetches (universe/ratings/short-interest feasible now, on the worker) are the next step.** Phase 1 scan = **F404** (gated on Phase 0). Universe full-fetch also advances **F385** (creates the broad worker cache).
- **[F403](TODO-archive.md#f403) full short-interest panel produced + verified on the worker (first Phase-0 data to land).** Ran the full FINRA ingest on strategylab-worker via `run_phase0_ingest.py` (worker-preflight green: yfinance/pandas/pyarrow + outbound net to yfinance/NASDAQ/FINRA all OK). Result: `event_panel.parquet` (53MB) + `daily_panel.parquet` (153MB), **2,902,231 event rows × 42,605 tickers, 2018-02-15→2024-12-31** (158 of 166 settlement files; 8 don't exist on FINRA, skipped). Metadata stamps verified against the real artifact: `survivorship=survivors-only`, `pit_field=dissemination_date` (settlement +7 business days), `coverage_start=2018-02-15`, and the corrected `exchange_coverage_note`. **This run is the production-scale proof of the C-001 fix** — `build_daily_panel`, which crashed on any multi-date ticker, forward-filled thousands of multi-date tickers across 6 years into the 153MB daily panel without error. **Still running:** universe price fetch (~12.4k symbols × 10y, the multi-hour long pole, into the worker price cache). **Queued behind universe** (shares yfinance, avoid throttle): ratings (stocks-only ~7k); news stays the F405 liquid-universe subset.
- **[F400](TODO-archive.md#f400) universe price fetch COMPLETE on the worker (~3h, exit 0).** 12,384 symbols → **9,222 with real price data** (3,162 empty = SPAC units/warrants/new-listings with no 2015–2024 history, cleanly skipped; **0 errors**) — roughly 2× the old ~4,700 liquid universe. SNAP (excluded by UNIVERSE_V2) is now cached for 2015–2024 → widen verified at the data level. Manifest stamps confirmed in the artifact (survivorship-only, `in_liquid_universe_v1` honest rename). Advances **F385** (broad worker cache now staged). Minor follow-up noted: the fetch ran `cache_hit=0` (re-fetched already-cached names instead of reusing — harmless for a one-time build, wasteful on re-runs). With universe done and yfinance free, **ratings launched** (stocks-only, manifest-sourced) and **news launched in parallel** (GDELT ≠ yfinance, no contention; bounded to the in_liquid_universe_v1 subset per F405). Driver gained a `--source news` branch.
- **Watchdog round on the two parallel fetches — killed news (confirmed non-viable), fixed + relaunched ratings.** Caught both running ~10× too slow within the hour. **News:** ~38 of 4,204 tickers in the first hour under sustained GDELT throttling → ~44h projected even at the bounded subset → killed. This is the empirical proof that the per-ticker GDELT DOC API is dead at scale (PERF-01 confirmed); news is the one Phase-0 panel not produced, and **F405** (GKG bulk) is now its real blocker (F402/F405 notes updated). **Ratings:** only reached the "C"s in an hour (~10h projected) because the retry loop retried the definitive `404 "no fundamentals"` responses (most small-caps have no analyst coverage) 3× with backoff — the dominant cost. Fixed per PERF-02's actual intent: `fetch_ticker_ratings` now **raises `RatingsFetchError` on a transient error** (caller retries) and returns **None for genuine no-coverage** (definitive, no retry); the loop separates `n_tickers_empty` (no coverage, not a failure) from `n_tickers_failed` (transient). Probe still 5/5; relaunched — each no-coverage ticker now logs once and the run drops from ~10h to ~2h. (Post-review fix, empirically verified in production: single 404 per ticker.)
- **[F401](TODO-archive.md#f401) ratings panel COMPLETE + PIT-cleaned (3rd Phase-0 panel; ~1h45m after the fix).** Final: **411,136 event rows / 313,201 agg rows, 4,396 of 7,053 stocks with ratings (2,657 no-coverage, 0 failed)** — the retry-fix accounting clean (no-coverage ≠ failure). On the metadata verify I caught a `coverage_end: 2026-10-05` (4 months future) — **1 future-dated row** in 411k (AMR, Jefferies reiterate, a yfinance data error) that the 25-ticker probe couldn't see. A future-dated rating action is a PIT look-ahead landmine for the discovery scan, so: added a **PIT guard** to `fetch_ticker_ratings` (drop `date > now`) for future refreshes, and **patched the shipped panel in place** on the worker (drop the row, rebuild agg+meta, no 2h re-fetch) → max_date now 2026-06-08. **Phase 0 is now 3 of 4 panels produced + verified** (universe / ratings / short interest); F402 news is the lone gap, honestly blocked on F405 (GKG bulk). The foundation is usable for Phase 1 (F404) on the three panels.

## 2026-06-08

- **[F397](TODO-archive.md#f397) Premise idea-history + dispositions — the workbench now remembers.** John flagged that his first premise "wasn't saved in the GUI" and asked for a history of ideas + *why* they were rejected, to revisit later. Root cause (no data loss — the store persists + the list fetches): the library showed only a status chip, never the outcome/why, and didn't live-refresh out-of-band changes. Brainstormed (both machine-outcome + user-note; edit-in-place + clone-to-sharpen) → built via orchestrator cycle. Per idea the library now shows a derived **machine outcome** ("UNTESTABLE — 0 events" / "failed: …" / explore stats) + a **disposition** the user sets (active / parked-needs-data / parked-sharpen / rejected / promising) + a note, plus **lineage** (`derived_from`). New endpoints `PUT /{id}/disposition` + `POST /{id}/duplicate` (clone-to-sharpen — deep-copies the spec, fresh history, original untouched), list extended with disposition/derived_from/machine_outcome + `?disposition=` filter; UI gains the chip+filter, a Disposition block, a Duplicate button, a lineage backlink, and a **15s live-refresh** (the actual fix for "looked unsaved"). Existing premises migrate via lazy-defaults on load. Tier B review-wave (correctness/data-integrity/typescript + adversarial): all core behavior PASS-confirmed; 8 fixes incl. a P1 silent note-truncation (now 422 + maxLength) and a P2 clone-`spec_version` phantom-history bug. 154 tests, build + 4-gate probe pass, runtime data git-untouched. Deferred P3 polish → **F398**.
- **[F395](TODO-archive.md#f395) Insider-SELL dose instrument (`s1_score`) — first user-driven premise on the workbench.** John authored a premise via the Desk ("small/mid-cap stocks dip when insider *selling* surfaces, recover in 4-6 weeks") — but the only wired dose was `r1_score` (insider *buying*, transactionCode P). The bounded-vocabulary guarantee correctly REFUSED to formalize a sell premise against a buy score (F338 discipline catching a new-instrument gap before any misleading number). So built the sell instrument: `s1_dose.py` as a faithful mirror of `r1_dose` (reuses its 10b5-1/shares/close/window helpers; new S+disposed parse + `build_s1_events`), same frozen score with D = discretionary (non-10b5-1) sale dollars; run-service builder-dispatch (`_DOSE_BUILDERS`); a `max_market_cap` universe floor (in-builder, since shares aren't available at floor-check time; added to `build_r1_events` too); F338 probe (`probe_s1_dose.py`). Methodology (mirror-r1 to minimize novel choices): 10b5-1 sales excluded by default (planned=noise, discretionary=signal), dose-id implies side. Orchestrator cycle: explore → implement → verify (162 tests + probe) → Tier C review-wave (correctness/data-integrity/python + adversarial — all core behavior PASS-confirmed) → 6 fixes → a 7th surgical fix the orchestrator caught on verify-against-reality: the 10b5-1 diagnostic had been over-corrected into an under-count and the probe's Anchor B had degraded into a tautology with contradictory text — fixed to a real `n_10b51_sales_seen_total > 0` parse-time check (562 planned sales on 2019 real data). Final: 163 tests, probe 16/16, FDR ledger untouched. **Scientific signal for the premise:** ~562 planned vs ~11 discretionary sell-events in 2019 → discretionary insider sales are ~50× rarer than planned; likely thin/underpowered (R-2 family) — filed **F396** (power census before believing any s1 verdict). Next: formalize + preview John's premise against s1_score.
- **[F390](TODO-archive.md#f390) Desk Premise Workbench — Phase 3: Desk tab + Premises workbench UI (frontend) — workbench now end-to-end.** New **"The Desk"** tab (4th tab in App.tsx, beside Chart/Live Trading/Discovery) with a Premises master-detail workbench (Inbox/Playbooks/Tracking subpanes stubbed "later"). The plain-English loop: author an idea (free-text + optional guided prompts folded into premise_text) → "what the AI will test" readback + a "▸ show runnable details" fold over the raw spec + a manual JSON spec editor (so it works with the agent operator OR by hand — option A, no in-app AI in v1) → run controls (fast preview / full explore) with status polling → plain-English verdict → a graduate-to-confirm gate honestly labelled "frozen + queued for OOS confirm; the real confirm run is a separate step (F393)." Typed client `api/premises.ts` over the F389 endpoints. Two small backend additions the UI needed: `POST /api/premises/{id}/submit` (draft→awaiting_formalization — the agent-operator queue trigger) and `save_spec` auto-advancing through legal state edges to spec_ready; plus `GET /api/premises?status=` so the agent can poll its queue. Built via orchestrator cycle: explore → implement → verify (build + 4-gate verify-batch render probe + 120 tests) → Tier B review-wave (correctness/typescript/agent-native + adversarial) → 12 fixes → re-verify (build, 123 tests, all 4 gates, ledger untouched). Review's best catches: verdict typed `string` but stored as a dict (rendered "[object Object]" — fixed with a VerdictPayload type + a VerdictDisplay table), a `'failed'` run-status the UI couldn't color, and the missing queue-status filter (agent-native parity gap). Deferred: F394 now also covers server-restart run recovery (AN-5). **The premise workbench is now usable end-to-end** — author, formalize (agent/manual), explore-backtest on the worker, read a plain-English verdict, iterate, and freeze+queue for confirm — all on cached data, with the FDR discipline gate structurally enforced (explore never touches the real ledger).
- **[F389](TODO-archive.md#f389) Desk Premise Workbench — Phase 2: run service + explore/confirm gate (backend).** Run service (`premise_run.py`): local fast-preview (reduced universe/window) + full-explore dispatched to the worker via the F387 probe → `worker-dispatch.sh` → `worker-status.sh` sentinel poll; async job-state, one-active-job-per-premise guard. Subprocess worker entry (`premise_run_worker.py`), 8 agent-native FastAPI endpoints (`routes/premises.py`, registered in main.py — same surface the UI and the agent operator both call), and an operator runbook. **The discipline gate:** explore/preview runs ALWAYS pass `fdr_ledger_path=None` (never touch the real ledger); `graduate-to-confirm` freezes spec_hash + runs a power_audit pre-check + store-wide hash idempotency + transitions `explored→awaiting_confirm`. **Key methodology call (for John's review):** v1 graduate does NOT run a backtest, never passes the real ledger, and cannot reach `confirmed` — the real out-of-sample confirm run (which writes `fdr_ledger.json`) is deferred to **F393** because the confirm-window semantics (2021–2024 window, 2025+ reserve, era breakdown, multiplicity) are a research-methodology decision, not an autonomous build call. The gate *structure* is fully built + tested; only the irreversible ledger-writing execution waits on sign-off (rationale: `.run/F389/decisions.md`). Built via orchestrator cycle: explore → implement → verify (42 tests, ledger-untouched invariant) → **Tier C review-wave (5 personas: correctness/security/reliability/data-integrity/python + adversarial verify)** → 12 fixes → re-verify (114 tests F388+F389, ledger untouched, shell scripts `bash -n` clean). Review's best catches: a dead idempotency guard (wrong field name, 3 reviewers), a `save_spec`-on-`awaiting_confirm` stale-hash path, DONE-without-verdict / STATUS=TIMEOUT leaving jobs stuck, and `premise_id` path-traversal + unquoted SSH interpolation (fixed with a `^p-[0-9a-f]{8}$` format guard on every route + quoting in `worker-status.sh`/`worker-dispatch.sh`). Adversarial pass CONFIRMED the never-touch-real-ledger invariant intact (SEC-11 P0). New follow-ups: **F393** (gated OOS confirm), **F394** (run-service hardening).
- **[F388](TODO-archive.md#f388) Desk Premise Workbench — Phase 1: PremiseSpec data model + stream registry (backend).** First build phase of The Desk (design: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-08-desk-premise-workbench-design.md; plan: docs/plans/2026-06-08-desk-premise-workbench-plan.md). Five new modules: a vocab-bounded `PremiseSpec` (Pydantic, validators reject any unregistered stream/dose or undeclared `event_filter` key) + structural-only `spec_hash()` (excludes prose, canonicalizes int/float + sorts scalar lists so semantically-identical specs share one FDR test identity); a `Stream` protocol + registry with a `form4` stream (bare `iter_form4_events` path — dose computation deferred to F389); a pure `spec → EventStudyConfig` compiler (`CompileResult`) that hardcodes the explore ceiling (never spec-driven — anti-p-hacking); a JSON premise store (`premises.json`) with a 7-state machine + atomic persistence. **No UI, no runs yet.** Built via the orchestrator cycle: explore (sonnet, interfaces mapped) → implement → verify (37 tests + F338 real-data probe 7/7: AAPL 78 events, all-2019 103,782) → review-wave (3 personas + adversarial verify) → 13 fixes → re-verify (48 tests, probe holds). Review caught a real empty-registry validation bypass (P1), spec_hash int/float + list-order non-canonicalization (would have let reworded specs dodge FDR multiplicity), a version-accounting bug (3 reviewers), and load-path data-loss risks (silent-empty-on-corrupt → overwrite) — all fixed. Four orchestrator overrides applied during the build (structural-only hash; no stub vocab keys; tests-in-research-dir convention; bare form4 path). New follow-ups: **F391** (typed event_filter value schemas, pairs with F389), **F392** (reviewer polish).

- **[F348](TODO-archive.md#f348) Fundamental-surprise payload layer shipped** (`backend/research/fundamental_surprise.py`) — the dose-score enabler for the F370 earnings-surprise charter. **Plain English:** for any company quarterly filing (10-Q/10-K), it measures how that quarter changed versus the *same company's own past* — revenue growth vs a year ago, whether that growth is speeding up, margin shift, share dilution, cash-vs-earnings quality — using **only what was public on the filing date** (no consensus estimates, no look-ahead). Eight signed deltas; every one reads `None` (not 0) when the data isn't there. Two layers: a pure per-event primitive `compute_surprise_payload(cik, as_of)` that F370/F347 will import, and an enumerator that turns the 10-Q/10-K stream into harness-ready events. **The F338 real-data probe earned its keep** — run on the worker, it caught what the 28 hermetic unit tests couldn't: the "disk-only" code was actually fetching live from SEC on a cache-miss (non-deterministic, mutates shared cache); fixed to a disk-only loader (PROBE-01). Final probe **5/5 PASS** — AAPL FY2019 face-valid (revenue −3.2% YoY matching reality, 21% net margin, −6.4% buyback), and the look-ahead anchor turned positive: **8 same-quarter restatements filed after the event were correctly excluded** (real-data proof the point-in-time guard holds). **review-wave** (5 personas + adversarial verify, 11 agents) confirmed 5 P1s — `_val_at_end` now picks latest-filed (amendment-vintage symmetry), margin guards reject negative prior revenue, the module is decoupled from premise_power_census, the probe's own look-ahead check was itself buggy (flagged raw restatements the code never used) and is rewritten to reconstruct the code's actual selection. 40 tests; full suite 1418. **Unblocks F370.** New TODOs: F376 (worker-dispatch backend-root sync gap — the probe first crashed on a stale `fileutil.file_lock`), F377 (NUMERIC_KEYS dedup), F378 (enumerator double-scan), F379 (strengthen weak assertions).

- **[F369](TODO-archive.md#f369) Premise power census shipped** (`backend/research/premise_power_census.py` + spec + report, run on the 14900k worker). Read-only feasibility pass — counts events + measures forward-return dispersion to predict MDE per premise family; draws no FDR alpha, believes no signal. F338-anchored: reproduces R-1b exactly (n=4,245, std 23.39pp, one-sample MDE 1.006pp, dose-gap 3.37pp) before any family number is read. **Finding: the power ceiling that sank R-1b is rare-event-specific.** High-frequency public filings are ~3× more testable at the same ~24pp dispersion — PEAD 10-Q/10-K (n 13,828, MDE **0.56pp**), 8-K 2.02 earnings (n 13,067, MDE **0.58pp**), 8-K 5.02 (MDE 0.77pp) all clear the 1.0pp floor as a binary test → **next charter should come from the PEAD/8-K family** (F370). R-2 will be UNTESTABLE (447 events, MDE 4.63pp — census saved the blind run, F372). R-1b's "calmer universe" fix is dead (edge in small-caps +7.6pp, gone in large-caps −0.18pp). **Two MDEs framing promoted to PROGRAM.md:** one-sample vs dose-gap (~3× harder); a premise can be testable as binary yet not as dose-response. **Honesty win:** the uniform ~+1.5pp filing mean-excess is a size/survivorship baseline artifact, NOT signal — proven structural by a before/after test (fixing a real after-hours look-ahead bug, COR-02, moved means <0.1pp); power numbers trustworthy, mean flagged do-not-believe pending an F338 PIT-benchmark probe (F371). **Reviews:** correctness (1 P0/3 P1) + Python (2 P0/6 P1); holistic fixer applied COR-01/02/03/04 + shared-helper consolidation + funnel counters, anchors stayed green; re-run on worker. **Infra:** all compute now goes to the worker (John's standing rule) — built `bin/worker-run.sh` to kill the cmd→WSL→bash quote trap; pre-flight data manifest before launch (F368); worker results byte-identical to local (determinism, cf. F357).

That made a full census possible instead of a 20-sample: 19/19 EDGAR web index stamps read exactly 19:00/20:00 ET *on the filing date* = 00:00:00 UTC next day — JSON and web agree to the second, DST split (winter 19:00 / summer 20:00) perfectly consistent. Event ET date == filing date, so the feared 1-day look-ahead doesn't occur even counterfactually. No exclusion, no clamp, no code change; `adt_midnight_utc` stays as provenance. ### Phase-2 full-universe verdict — the filter does NOT beat the null (the gate worked)

- **Full write-up / fresh-session pickup doc:** [docs/plans/poor-mans-bloomberg-phase2-run1.md](plans/poor-mans-bloomberg-phase2-run1.md) — verdict, autopsy, bug ledger, rerun protocol (§8 is the next-step sequence), idea bank, data locations.

- **The run:** 8,909 names × 36 quarterly as-of dates (2015–2023), locked config, 105 min, 1,039 fetch failures (12%), 3,156 overlapping events suppressed, 2,064 unique tickers, 3.9GB EDGAR cache built (one-time; reruns are minutes).
- **The verdict:** signal 44/125 = **35.2%** [27.4–43.9] vs null 1,384/3,034 = **45.6%** [43.9–47.4] at +50%/12m touch. The full-screen picks did WORSE than merely-washed-out names; signal CI entirely at/below null. Mean net +8.8% is 2015–23 beta, not edge. **Phases 3–5 stay gated; $0 spent on UI for a non-edge — the spec §8 gate did exactly its job.**
- **The autopsy (miss-list top 10 told the story):** GPRO-2015/-54%, ENPH/CRSR/BOOM-2023, oilfield trio 2018-19 — all decelerating former highfliers whose price crashed before their filings, not recovering troughs. **Price leads, filings trail**: conditioning on good trailing fundamentals after a crash selects names where the bad news hasn't finished arriving. Root cause is a spec-conformance bug, not a thesis kill: spec §4 says revenue "has TURNED positive" (sign change); the implementation accepted any ≥0 YoY run → F326. Killed a proxy, not the thesis — the real test is the F319+F326+F321 rerun.
- **The ENPH coda:** the screen's #10-conviction pick (2023-11, −36% in window) is John's best long of 2026 — he manually waited for the actual turn the way Stage-2 + the sign-change gate are meant to. "Right name, wrong clock" is a distinct failure mode from "wrong name" → F328 miss-list horizon diagnostic; ENPH 2023→2026 point-in-time timeline is the case-study vignette after F326.
- **Live ticker session (7 names through the pillars with John):** NKE (washed-out ✓, GM 0.53pp from candidacy), EL (first inflecting ✓), INTC (+516% off low = the chase, rejected), TGT/MRNA/AMC/PTON each rejected for a different pillar — archetype separation works. Caught two systemic duds via positive controls that review+tests missed: conviction pillar dead (4 stacked bugs: EFTS int-CIK silent zero, missing dateRange=custom→500, wrong response fields, wrong Form4 index URL → F321) and dual-class shares P/S gap (PTON/NKE/EL → F322). **Thrice-proven session lesson: graceful degradation without positive controls manufactures silence that looks like data.**
- **Gate design review (with John):** keep binary membership (validation sets + auditability), add zero-knob transparency — per-gate margins + near-miss "forming" tier + inflection stage labels (F324), dose-response cohorts as harsher falsification (F325), F317 sticky-membership design note (Gate-1 below-MA contradicts Stage-2 reclaim trigger). Also filed F319 (junk null), F320 (derived fundamentals cache; 134MB→3.9GB growth measured live), F323 (archive-todo anchor ordering), F327 (null return distribution). Explainer for non-market readers shipped: docs/explainers/turnaround-screen.md.

### Turnaround screen ("Poor Man's Bloomberg") Phases 1+2 — F311 + F312, full orchestrator cycle

- **[F311](TODO-archive.md#f311)** Stage-1 universe filter: `backend/edgar.py` (SEC EDGAR client — rate-limited single-chokepoint `_get` with retry/backoff, atomic on-disk TTL cache, XBRL quarterly extraction with 4-tag revenue fallback + Q4-from-annual derivation, Form 4 XML buy/sell parsing) + `backend/turnaround.py` (pure point-in-time filter engine, `as_of` everywhere, injectable `bars_loader`, cheap-first funnel: price/volume gate → washed-out → XBRL inflection → valuation+conviction) + `routes/turnaround.py` (async scan with status polling, persisted watchlist). Spec: `docs/plans/poor-mans-bloomberg-spec.md`.
- **[F312](TODO-archive.md#f312)** Validation hook: `backend/turnaround_validation.py` — quarterly as-of historical scans through the same filter code path, ONE network fetch per symbol (memoized full-span loader), entry/exit with slippage+commission, hit = net return ≥ +50% within 12m, Wilson CIs, hit rate vs null (washed-out-only names), miss list with real composite scores, per-ticker overlap cooldown (pseudo-replication guard), survivorship warning with asymmetric-bias note. Conviction skipped in validation by design (additive-only, never gatekeeps).
- **Build shape:** 1 explore+spec (sonnet) with orchestrator addendum correcting 4 brief flaws (per-as-of refetch → memoized loader; Form 4 transaction codes aren't in submissions JSON → XML fetch; conviction-in-validation unrunnable → skip; FilterParams dataclass → BaseModel); 3 parallel impl lanes (zero file overlap, interfaces pinned in brief); cross-lane gate caught lane B's module-level `sys.modules` stubs poisoning the whole pytest session at collection (38 false failures) — fixed inline.
- **Review (Tier C, 5 personas ∥ live verification):** 52 findings, 8 P0 — best catches: calendar-vs-trading-day lookback windows (3yr low was actually 4.3yr, 2 reviewers), `date.today()` in conviction accessors = lookahead for any historical as_of, exception→pass valuation gate (data gaps would silently inflate the candidate list), non-atomic Form 4 cache (torn file = poisoned forever), check-then-act races + stuck-"running" status on worker crash. 38 fixed via two parallel fixers (disjoint file sets, pinned conviction-accessor interface); 6 deferred → F313–F316 + gated F317/F318. Orchestrator catches: silent FilterParams→defaults fallback, missing universe cap on validation (10k names ≈ hours), `backend/data/turnaround/` unignored (MB-scale cache one `git add` from the repo).
- **Live verification (concurrent with review):** 150-name scan + 300-name 2022–23 validation ran end-to-end; EDGAR XBRL plumbing proven against reality (AAPL companyfacts → inflecting=True, rev +16% YoY, GM +1.7pp). **Best catch of the session — by positive control, not review:** both live runs returned 0 candidates, which initially looked legitimate (A-ticker SPAC/ETF universe); driving PTON@2022-09-15 through `evaluate_washed_out` directly exposed a TypeError on EVERY live symbol — `_fetch()` frames carry a tz-aware ET DatetimeIndex, all synthetic test fixtures were tz-naive, and `run_filter`'s per-symbol try/except swallowed the crash into silent zeros. Fixed in `_df_up_to` (strip tz once, wall-clock preserved) + tz-aware regression tests. Lesson reinforced: a screen that can silently return "no candidates" needs a known-positive control, every time. 878 backend tests green (+91 new); all verify-batch gates PASS.
- Phases 3–5 (catalyst monitor, screen UI, bot graduation) gated on F312 results per spec §8 — "Phase 2 is the gate."

## 2026-06-04

### A8 rework — auto-downsample moved to pure render-layer resampling (user-driven, full cycle)

- **Why:** user observed the refetch-based switch pulls coarse bars from the provider — far slower than acceptable on big ranges (well beyond seconds). Reworked to client-side resampling: `aggregateBars`/`aggregateLineSeries` in chartUtils.ts, `autoRenderInterval` state + `effectiveInterval` derivation in Chart.tsx, SubPane `bucketSecs` prop with shared bucket floors (pane alignment invariant). Manual "Aggregate ▾" keeps the backend path (provider-official bars); only AUTO is client-side. **Measured: 521ms zoom-out switch / 308ms restore (mostly the 150ms debounce) vs multi-second refetches.**
- **Design dividend:** the entire async race class from the first cycle (pending guards, reload trap, error resets) was deleted — App.tsx lost `handleAutoInterval`/`autoIntervalActive`/conditional persist; **[F308](TODO-archive.md#f308) closed as structurally dissolved** (no fetch window = no stranded-view race).
- **Review (same 3-persona panel):** 15 findings, 3 P1 — `'1d'` missing from chartUtils `INTERVAL_SECONDS` (markers/regime/signals silently drop at the 1d render tier; locked with snap-invariant tests), restore-path re-entering the evaluator with only incidental convergence (in-flight guard on `autoRangeRef`), `any`-erasure in the new utils. One holistic fixer; 389/389 tests.
- **Live catch (orchestrator):** the checkbox-disable path swapped data without capturing the time window — view collapsed to 8.9% of extent and made re-enable a silent no-op (window fell below threshold). Fixed with capture-before-clear, guarded to active-only so an unconsumed `autoRangeRef` can't wedge the evaluator. Full toggle cycle verified: 882-day span constant across coarsen → uncheck → recheck.
- **Filed:** F310 (one-frame pane misalignment during swap, RACE-04, acceptable-as-is).

### A8 off-screen downsampling — auto view-interval at wide zoom (full cycle + live browser verification)

- **[A8](TODO.md#a8) sub-item shipped:** auto-downsampling rides the existing A8b/A8d `viewInterval` pipeline (backend resamples OHLCV + indicators — no client-side aggregators, the explore brief's dual-path Steps 1/5/6 were cut at the fork). `evaluateAutoInterval` pure helper in `intervals.ts` (8000-on/6000-off thresholds in BASE-equivalent bars, 150ms debounce, `pendingSince` timestamp guard with 5s safety valve), `onAutoInterval` → App.tsx, `getVisibleRange` capture/restore across the swap, "Auto (1h)" header badge, `INTERVAL_SECS` consolidated to one export.
- **Orchestrator catch at spec:** the brief's hysteresis pseudocode compared raw logical span — after a 5m→15m switch the same window spans ⅓ the bars (< off-threshold) → instant restore loop. Fixed via base-equivalent normalization (D3) before any code was written.
- **Review (correctness + julik-races + kieran-ts, effort-capped):** 19 findings, 4 P1 — guard wedged forever on empty/failed refetch (3 reviewers converged), reload trap (persisted coarse viewInterval + lost flag = stuck UI), reverse-zoom snap-back, plus tier re-escalation gap. One holistic fixer; 40 new vitest cases; 365/365 pass.
- **Live-browser catch (the big one):** lw-charts' default `timeScale.minBarSpacing: 0.5` clamps zoom-out at ~2× pane width in bars (~2.3K) — the 8000-bar threshold was UNREACHABLE and the feature could never fire. Unit tests, build, and render-probe were all green; only live interaction exposed it. Fixed with `minBarSpacing: 0.01` on all three chart instances (SubPane too — the default would have silently refused synced wide ranges and desynced panes). Browser-verified end-to-end on AAPL alpaca 5m 2.4yr (47,255 bars): zoom-out → 3,636 pts (13×, tier-2 1h direct), "Auto (1h)" badge, persisted viewInterval stays 5m, zoom-in restores full resolution with the time window preserved, mount-time auto-coarsen works cold. Evidence: `.run/A8-downsample/browser-assertions.json` + screenshots.
- **Filed:** F308 (stranded view 1-of-4 on first-ever switch — diagnosis hook left in place), F309 (promote the zoom recipe to a scripted probe check per F298). Viewport-only rendering remains the open A8 sub-item.
- **Follow-up (user request, same day):** auto-downsampling is now an "Auto" checkbox in the chart header (default on, persisted as `autoDownsample` in settings). Unchecking while auto-active restores base resolution immediately; the evaluation is gated via `autoIntervalEnabledRef`. Re-enabling while already zoomed wide invokes the evaluation directly through `autoEvalRef` — a fake setVisibleLogicalRange "nudge" doesn't work because lw-charts skips no-change range assignments (found live: the first nudge implementation silently did nothing). Browser-verified all four toggle states; 365/365 tests.

### TODO.md redesign (TODO-REDESIGN) — research → plan → migration, one orchestrated cycle

- **Research (3 agents):** 87.2% of TODO.md's 140KB was checked-item residue; F### confirmed as a *section* problem, not an ID problem (category-in-ID is a documented anti-pattern — GitHub/Beads both rejected it); prior art unanimous that active/done must physically split at this scale. Tooling map: 32 coupling points, JOURNAL anchors + git-log archaeology pin the ID scheme immutable.
- **Shipped:** `bin/archive-todo.py` (write-before-delete, idempotent, fence-aware JOURNAL link rewriting, month-merge); sync-todo-index v6 (structure-agnostic, index derived from real H2 sections, anchor dedupe, shared ID_PAT with pre-commit — fixed the latent narrow-regex bug); close-batch.py inserts by bucket tag into the new H2 sections.
- **Migrated:** TODO.md 279 lines/144KB → open-only bucket structure (~60 lines body, 18KB); 190 checked items → TODO-archive.md (anchors preserved); 130 JOURNAL links rewritten; E1–E4 + C30 evicted to IDEAS.md with provenance (IDs retired); 10 condition-gated items → `## Deferred (gated)` with explicit `[gated: …]` tags. ID inventory: 220 → 215, 5 intentional retirements, 0 losses.
- **Policy:** F### = monotonic item counter forever; bucket tags carry categorization; new prefix letters banned; archive step joins session close; builder skips [gated] items and picks Up Next → bucket order.
- **Review (capped per F302, 1 persona):** 6 findings — 1 P0 (close-batch.py still targeted the dissolved F-section; would have broken the next overnight closeout), 3 P1 (archive edge cases: standalone anchors, blank-line truncation, code-fence link rewrites), 1 P2 fixed, 1 P2 rejected (gated items count as open by design). The P0 validates keeping a correctness pass even on "just tooling" batches. Effort cap held: 40 tool calls vs the 55 uncapped equivalent last batch.
- **Orchestrator catches:** index table regenerated by ID prefix instead of section (wrong index worse than none), duplicate anchor injection — both found by orchestrator eyeball between migration and review, fixed before review even landed.

The growth drivers that remain: inline browser triage (CDP focus forensics — judgment work, correctly inline), review synthesis, and user discussion. Cache hit 100% at finish; 43.9M total tokens is cache-read dominated.

### 8-item stress-test batch — probe catches its own canary lying

- **[F284] (P1, found live):** F140's walk-through clicked SignalScanner's "Save Watchlist" and **wiped the real watchlist file** — `saveWatchlist` POSTed legacy `{symbols}`, Pydantic `extra="ignore"` dropped it, an empty state persisted; `fetchWatchlist` read `data.symbols` (always `[]`), so the breakage was invisible. Same disease as the AddBotRequest silent-drop in Key Bugs Fixed. Data recovered from a Safari tab's localStorage mirror (backend stopped first so the mirror couldn't be clobbered — the destructive overwrite path was identified before asking the user to act). Fix: `extra="forbid"`, flatten read, RMW save that throws on GET failure, `_watchlist_lock` (race corroborated by 3 of 4 reviewers), seed legacy-shape normalization (killed a latent 422-on-every-load loop), mirror guard. Live re-verified post-fix with user authorization: groups survive the scanner save; a failed save leaves disk untouched; red `!` + tooltip confirmed.
- **[F285] (postmortem):** recovery was luck (an open Safari tab) — now `atomic_write_text` keeps a rotating `.bak` (default depth 1, watchlist depth 2, `.env.bak` chmod 0600). Snapshot-before-destructive-verification added to the live-browser protocol.

K4's regex "tightening" that would have blinded the F283 guard to prose notes — orchestrator domain knowledge), 1 deferred with rationale (DI-04 mirror-guard inverse bug is cosmetic-during-outage; defense-in-depth wins), 1 self-cleared. Sharpest catches: `.env.bak` world-readable secrets (DI-01), transient-GET-failure re-wipe in the "fixed" save path (K-02).

## 2026-05-25

### NodeBuilder: UX polish + Houdini-style interaction patterns

Long interactive session on `feat/node-strategy-builder-t1-t2`. User-driven loop: identify a sluggishness, ship a fix, verify, iterate. Eight commits.

- **[F260](TODO-archive.md#f260)** Per-node `rfNode`/`rfEdge` memoization in `Canvas.tsx`. The previous edit-mode perf fixes (13bc6e7) memoized the rfNodes *array* but every store mutation rebuilt every node's `data` reference, so `React.memo` on custom renderers invalidated for all N nodes per single-node change. Now: a per-node useRef cache keyed by node id holds a signature of the node's mutable fields; when the sig matches, the SAME rfNode reference (and `data` reference) is reused. **Render count goes from N to 1 per single-node mutation.** Same pattern for `rfEdges`. (a6e5a44)
- **[F261](TODO-archive.md#f261)** "Edit this graph" button in `NodeBuilder.tsx` toolbar — clones the read-only auto-render graph into the Zustand store via existing `loadFromAutoRender()` so the user keeps their work and can iterate. Visible only when an auto-render graph exists and edit mode is not active. (a6e5a44)
- **[F262](TODO-archive.md#f262)** Live drag updates. React Flow was running in a broken half-controlled mode: `nodes={rfNodes}` passed without `onNodesChange` — RF's internal node state was being overwritten by the prop on every parent render. Drag visually snapped only at mouseup. Fix: introduced a local mirror (`localNodes`/`localEdges` useState) that RF drives via `onNodesChange`/`onEdgesChange` + `applyNodeChanges`/`applyEdgeChanges`; store stays authoritative via the existing drag-end/connect/delete handlers + a useEffect that syncs from store. Memo arrays are reference-stabilized so the sync doesn't fire when nothing changed. (61795ed; TDZ fix on `prevRfNodesRef` follow-up in 2953b29)
- **[F263](TODO-archive.md#f263)** Cursor semantics scoped under `.nodebuilder-root`: empty pane → grab/grabbing, node → default arrow (grabbing while dragged), edge → pointer, handle/port → crosshair. Houdini-style: nodes feel like items, ports feel like connection sources, only empty pane invites panning. (61795ed)
- **[F264](TODO-archive.md#f264)** Houdini-style: drag a wire into empty space → TabMenu opens at cursor → pick a node → wire auto-connects in the direction you dragged. Wired `onConnectStart` to capture source node + handle type, `onConnectEnd` to detect drops on the pane. Direction-aware: source-handle drag → new node is target; target-handle drag → new node is source. Cleanup paths cover Esc / outside-click / drop-on-handle. (a461de8)
- **[F265](TODO-archive.md#f265)** Visible selection ring on selected nodes. RF's default `.react-flow__node.selected` style is a 0.5px dark outline — invisible on our dark theme. Selection IS firing (handlers run, store updates) but no visual feedback. Added an explicit 2px display-flag-colored ring + soft outer glow under `.nodebuilder-root`. (5897ba2)
- **[F266](TODO-archive.md#f266)** Selection-latency fix: suppress redundant local-mirror sync when only selection differs. After F262, clicking a node fired two renders — applyNodeChanges (immediate, paints selection) + a second render from the useEffect that re-replaced `localNodes` with store-derived `rfNodes` (same data, new refs). The second render+paint added perceptible lag on 120Hz displays. Fix: in the sync useEffect, compare curr↔rfNodes; if every element matches by id+data+position+draggable+type (only `selected` may differ) keep curr. (c97cfe8)
- **[F267](TODO-archive.md#f267)** Docs reorg: retired `docs/superpowers/` (plugin-specific holdover). ~45 plan + spec files moved via `git mv` into the existing `docs/plans/` and `docs/specs/` (plugin-agnostic, already where recent work lived). All cross-references in `.md` files updated via sed. CLAUDE.md "On Start" handoff contract updated to point at the new locations.
- **Verification**: all commits had `npm run build` clean + 55/55 nodebuilder tests pass. Browser-verified F262/F265/F266 via chrome-devtools-mcp (MutationObserver showed `.selected` class arriving on next frame, ~14.7ms). Multi-node drag perf verification deferred to user (synthetic seeding via Tab+Enter through CDP fights focus management; single-node measurement was already 60fps either way).
- **Handoff to next session**: parameter editing is the next chunk. Plan written at [`docs/plans/2026-05-25-node-builder-param-editing.md`](docs/plans/2026-05-25-node-builder-param-editing.md). Three in-session decisions captured (inline vs inspector, validation locus, auto-rerun vs manual) with recommendations. Tagged as **[F268](TODO-archive.md#f268)** `[next]`.

### NodeBuilder: inline param editing (F268)

Picked up the F268 handoff. Three decisions confirmed up-front: inline-first (inspector deferred), component-side coercion (backend validates on run), manual Run Backtest (no auto-rerun). Followed the planned implementation sequence exactly.

- **[F268](TODO-archive.md#f268)** Inline parameter editing on `IndicatorNode`. New pure op `updateNodeParams(graph, nodeId, partial)` in `operations.ts` mirrors `moveNode` (readOnly guard, shallow merge of partial into existing params, no-op on missing node). Three vitest cases added (15b merge, 15c readOnly throws, 15d missing-node no-op) — 28/28 pass. Store wiring is the standard 5-line pattern. `IndicatorNode` now hides the param suffix from the title when `editable=true` and renders a `ParamRows` block as `BaseNode` children: one row per param, numeric or text input depending on `typeof value`. Input commits on blur, reads from `e.target.value` directly (not draft state) so fast-type-then-blur can't lose the value to React batching. Enter blurs, ESC reverts. Container has `.nodrag .nopan` + `onPointerDown` stopPropagation so React Flow doesn't grab the cursor mid-type.
- **Browser-verified end-to-end** via chrome-devtools-mcp: clicked "Edit this graph", edited EMA(200) period input to 50, clicked Run Backtest. Inspected the resulting POST `/api/nodebuilder/backtest` payload — `"/ema_period_200":{...,"params":{"period":50},...}`. The store update flowed through `useNodeBuilderStore` → `handleRunBacktest` → backend. (`npm run build` clean.)
- **Verification false start worth recording**: my first synthetic test fired `props.onChange({target:{value:'50'}})` then `props.onBlur(...)` in the same task — React hadn't flushed the `setDraft` between, and my original `commit()` closed over stale `draft`. First backtest payload still had `period:200` (looked like a real bug). Hardened commit to read `e.target.value` directly, which is also correct for fast real-world typing, then re-verified via direct store-action invocation via fiber walk (period=999 reflected in DOM) and confirmed the round trip with the next POST showing `period:50`. Lesson: when a "verification" failure could be either a real bug or a test artifact, the harder fix that closes both is usually right.
- **Follow-ups filed (deliberately scoped out of F268):** **[F269](TODO-archive.md#f269)** extend to TickerNode/ComparisonNode/SettingsNode/LogicNode; **[F270](TODO-archive.md#f270)** extract shared `ParamRow.tsx` once a second renderer needs it; **[F271](TODO-archive.md#f271)** typed param schema from catalog so enums get `<select>` instead of free-text (RSI `type` is the canary); **[F272](TODO.md#f272)** inspector panel for nodes with >3 params (defer until F269+F271 expose actual cramping); **[F273](TODO-archive.md#f273)** visual feedback on invalid input (currently silently reverts).

### NodeBuilder: typed param schema → select inputs (F271)

Free-text inputs for enum params (RSI `type: sma|ema|wma`, ticker `interval`, ticker `source`) were the obvious next felt-quality gap. F271 makes them dropdowns.

- **[F271](TODO-archive.md#f271)** New `ParamTypeSpec` interface + optional `paramTypes?: Record<string, ParamTypeSpec>` on `NodeCatalogEntry`. Type values: `'number' | 'string' | 'select'`; select carries an `options` list. `ParamRow` renders a `<select>` for select-typed keys; it commits onChange immediately (no blur step — there's nothing to type) and falls back to inferring from `typeof value` when the catalog has no override. Unknown legacy values get prepended as the first option so loaded strategies don't silently lose data.
- Shared option lists `INTERVAL_OPTIONS` and `SOURCE_OPTIONS` live in `catalog.ts` so adding a provider/interval updates every catalog entry that exposes them in one place. Currently used only by `ticker` but the convention is established for future multi-ticker / multi-source nodes.
- **Wired for canaries:** ticker (`interval`, `source`), RSI (`type`). All four renderers (Indicator/Ticker/Comparison/Settings) now thread `catalog?.paramTypes` to `<ParamRows>`.
- **Browser-verified end-to-end:** changed RSI `type` sma→ema via the new dropdown, ticker `interval` 30m→1h, then Run Backtest. Returned 200 with both new values in the payload (`"type":"ema"`, `"interval":"1h"`) and produced 89 trades (sharpe -0.16, drawdown -63%; the strategy isn't profitable on this config but the data flow is what matters).
- **Follow-ups filed:** **[F276](TODO-archive.md#f276)** mechanical pass to add `paramTypes` to remaining catalog entries; **[F277](TODO-archive.md#f277)** test that walks the catalog and asserts every `paramTypes` key is also in `defaults.params` (would catch typos / drift).

### NodeBuilder: invalid-input feedback + paramTypes coverage + drift test (F273, F276, F277)

Three follow-ups from F271 shipped together. The thread is: ParamRow had a silent-revert path that hid bad input from the user; once that's surfaced (F273), and the catalog has full coverage (F276), the drift-guard test (F277) makes the invariant non-rotting.

- **[F273](TODO-archive.md#f273)** Invalid numeric input now shows a 1px `--nb-cat-rules` (red) border + outer glow + `title="Must be a number"` tooltip instead of silently reverting. State tracked per-row in `ParamRow` (`invalid: string | null`); auto-clears on next `onChange` (so red goes away as user starts fixing) and on ESC. Browser-verified both paths: `commit('abc')` → red+tooltip; `commit('21')` → clean. **Caveat surfaced and filed as F278:** `type="number"` strips any non-numeric char at the DOM layer (typing "1.2.3" yields `value=""`), so the visible-red path is actually only reachable via parse-failure cases like overflow (`1e9999` → `Infinity` → not finite). The silent-revert fallback handles the rest, but the F273 affordance fires less often than expected.
- **[F276](TODO-archive.md#f276)** Catalog `paramTypes` extended to every entry whose params contain numbers — MACD (fast/slow/signal), SMA/EMA/BB/ATR (period + stddev), all four comparisons (threshold), position_size (size), stop_loss (pct), slippage (bps), commission (per_share_rate + min_per_order). Numeric defaults are now canonically declared, enforced by F277.
- **[F277](TODO-archive.md#f277)** Drift-guard tests in `catalog.test.ts`: (a) every `paramTypes` key is also in `defaults.params` — catches renames/typos like `type` vs `kind`; (b) every select-typed entry has a non-empty options array — catches half-edited schema; (c) every numeric default has an explicit `paramTypes` entry — prevents the catalog from regressing back to typeof-inference for canonical params. 17/17 catalog tests pass.
- **One more follow-up filed:** **[F278](TODO-archive.md#f278)** switch numeric inputs to `type="text" inputMode="numeric"` so the DOM stops swallowing partial bad input and F273's red border fires for the everyday case, not just overflows.

### NodeBuilder: text+inputMode=decimal so red-border fires for everyday bad input (F278)

- **[F278](TODO-archive.md#f278)** Single-line swap in `ParamRow.tsx`: numeric inputs now use `type="text" inputMode="decimal"` instead of `type="number"`. Closes the loop on F273 — the visible-red path was previously only reachable via overflow-style invalidity because `type="number"` clobbered any unparseable char before our commit() could see it. Now typing `"abc"` keeps the bad value visible *and* triggers the red border + tooltip on blur. Browser-verified: `input.value === 'abc'`, red box-shadow, `title === 'Must be a number'`. Mobile gets the numeric keypad via `inputMode="decimal"`.

### Cache-economics-aware delegation rules (main branch)

- **[d6af5a8](https://github.com/jroxenhed/strategylab/commit/d6af5a8)** Updated CLAUDE.md "Subagent delegation rule" with a size threshold (work must exceed the ~5–15k system-prompt write cost) + new "Subagent cache-write cost" bullet documenting per-dispatch cost, same-type-within-5-min cache hits, and the long-running-subagent / parent-cache-aging trap. Overnight builder rule changed from "parallel review of 4–6 personas in one message" to "sequence within 5-min bursts" since wall-clock isn't a constraint overnight. "Just check one thing" anti-pattern flipped: reflexive subagent dispatch for trivial reads is wasteful when inline hits parent cache at 0.1×.
- **[00e7e07](https://github.com/jroxenhed/strategylab/commit/00e7e07)** Added "Review cost" line to the overnight Slack summary template so the user can see whether the sequential-burst rule is actually paying off. Empirical signal for the trial; revert is `git revert d6af5a8` if it doesn't.
- Memory updated: `feedback_model_routing.md` carries the new size-threshold framing so future sessions don't drift back to the old "haiku for every read" routing.

### Polygon.io data provider (separate branch)

- **[F251](TODO.md#f251)** on `feat/polygon-provider` (off main, not yet merged): `PolygonProvider` in `backend/shared.py` for 15y+ historical equity data. `/v2/aggs` endpoint, full interval map matching Alpaca, defensive rate-limit handling (Retry-After on 429, exponential backoff on 5xx, 5-retry cap), `next_url` pagination, RTH filter, no key-leak on auth errors. Registered conditionally on `POLYGON_API_KEY` env var. Sidebar source toggle gains "Polygon" button. Live fetch verification pending real API key. Follow-ups: **F251b** disk-persistent parquet cache (for Starter's 5 req/min), **F251c** polygon-crypto sibling.

Two real bugs caught during parity testing: MACD-wire-resolution at the comparison node (named `@macd_signal` wires were resolving to `@macd_line`), and ATR-trailing-stop needing an implicit ATR(14) when no ATR node was on the graph. Key fixes: applied wire.attr resolution to logic/terminal resolvers (same bug class as the MACD comparison fix, but the fix wasn't propagated to siblings); compile-time rejection of crossovers reading per-bar derived booleans (would silently never fire); permanent-error state when `kind=graph` + `graph=None` at tick time (was looping in MAX_CONSEC_ERRORS retry backoff).

Memoised `rfNodes`/`rfEdges`, replaced `NODE_CATALOG.find()` per-render with a module-load Map, switched `onMove` (per-pixel) → `onMoveEnd`, wrapped node renderers in `React.memo`. Improved but **not fully resolved** — see handoff doc.

### Post-merge interactive session — UX bug-fixes + server-side persistence

Long interactive session against `main` after Bundles D/E/F merged. User drove the loop, I shipped fixes one-by-one. 19 commits total; highlights:

- **Equity-curve ghosting**: `MacroEquityChart.tsx` still used the F218 trap (createChart with explicit `height` + external ResizeObserver firing `applyOptions`). Same fix as F218 — `autoSize: true`, drop the explicit height, drop the ResizeObserver. Browser-verified: canvas count stable through 6 forced resize cycles.
- **Watchlist Add-ticker dropdown** now uses the `useSearch` hook the sidebar already has — single-token input shows symbol + name suggestions with arrow-key nav and outside-click close; bulk-paste path (comma-separated) preserved.
- **Chart-panel collapse** (F248): the original `▾` glyph was 14×14 muted grey and invisible. Re-styled as a labeled "◂ Hide Chart" / "▸ Show Chart" button matching the Disable Chart button next to it. AND fixed the actual collapse: previous `display: none` left the Panel sized; now the chart `<Panel>` + horizontal `<Separator/>` are conditionally rendered inside the `<Group>`, with a key prop on Group to force layout reseat. Run-Backtest button climbs ~525px when collapsed.
- **Watchlist disappearing on every code update**: `WatchlistPanel.tsx` had `useState(emptyState)` + a load-on-mount useEffect paired with a save effect on `[state]`. First render captured empty, both effects fired in order — load `setState(loaded)` scheduled re-render but save then fired with the still-empty captured value and clobbered localStorage. Vite HMR triggered this every code save. Fixed by lazy-initializing state from `loadWatchlist()` via a ref so the component renders with the saved value from frame zero.
- **Server-side persistence for watchlist + saved strategies**: new `backend/routes/strategies.py` (GET / PUT / DELETE / POST seed) + extended `routes/trading.py` watchlist endpoints to the groups/ungrouped schema with auto-migration of the legacy `{symbols: []}` file. Frontend `watchlistStorage.ts` and `savedStrategies.ts` now fetch from the API with localStorage as a fallback mirror. `seedFromLocalStorage.ts` runs once per browser, pushing existing localStorage data to the server's `/seed` endpoints (idempotent — only writes when empty). 500/500 backend tests pass.
- **Wrong-URL deploy postmortem**: the server-side persistence shipped pointing at `/watchlist` + `/strategies` but FastAPI registered the routers under `/api/trading/watchlist` + `/api/strategies`. Every call 404'd silently. Combined with `SEED_ATTEMPTED` being set BEFORE the seed POSTs, the migration never ran and the flag was poisoned. Fixed all 6 endpoint URLs and bumped the seed-attempted key to `-v2` so every browser retries once. Seed flag now only marks attempted when both endpoints respond successfully. One follow-up commit caught savedStrategies.ts which was missed in the first URL-fix pass.
- **Single time axis across chart panes**: each of the 4 stacked panes (price/RSI/ADX/Stochastic) was rendering its own duplicate time axis row (~20-25px each). Hide on all panes except the bottom-most — `showTimeAxis` prop plumbed from `Chart.tsx` → `SubPanelEntry` → `SubPane`, with `applyOptions({visible})` for dynamic updates. ~60-75px of vertical real estate reclaimed for actual chart content.
- **Indicator color picker reflow**: color swatch was rendered between the drag handle and checkbox, shifting only color-supporting rows right and breaking checkbox column alignment. Moved to the right edge (before chevron/✕) and converted to a popover (outside-click + selection both close). Color removed from the inline expand panel — picker is exclusively a popover now.
- **Color picker — sync default + drop multi-line**: dot fell back to `PRESET_COLORS[0]` (red) while chart consumers used different fallbacks per type. New `DEFAULT_INDICATOR_COLOR` map in `shared/types/indicators.ts` matches the chart's per-type fallback; `createInstance` sets `inst.color` so dot and series agree from frame zero. MACD dropped from `SUPPORTS_COLOR` (multi-line — a single `inst.color` can't represent it); BB/ADX/Stochastic stay excluded; Volume + VWAP added.
- **Optimizer no-variance banner + pre-fetch**: when all 100 combos return the same metric value (because swept params don't influence any trade decision — e.g. a rule that never fires), the top-10 table looked broken with 10 indistinguishable rows. Added an orange banner explaining the cause. Also matched WFA's setup pattern: pre-fetch the DataFrame ONCE at the optimizer endpoint and pass `df=df` to `run_grid`, so per-combo cache lookup + defensive copy is skipped (~13× speedup on warm cache for the 5-combo synthetic test).
- **Optimizer/sweep param paths honor regime mode**: in regime/BOTH mode the engine reads `long_buy_rules`/`long_sell_rules`/`short_*_rules` — the bare `buy_rules`/`sell_rules` arrays are populated for UI symmetry but never consumed. The Param dropdown was emitting paths into the bare lists, so every sweep mutated arrays the backtest never read → zero variance every time. Fixed across `paramOptions.ts` (build / apply / group / shouldGroup), and `backend/routes/backtest_sweep.py` `_apply_param` collapsed 4 branches into one regex-driven path supporting `(long_|short_)?(buy|sell)_rule_<i>_(value|params_<key>)`.
- **Param picks reset on every Run Backtest** — the big one. Root cause: `StrategyBuilder.runBacktest` called `onResult(null)` at the top, which made App.tsx's `{backtestResult && <Results/>}` evaluate false → `<Results/>` unmounted in the same render cycle as the sub-panel state update. The save-on-paramRows useEffect never had a chance to commit. Fixed two layers:
  - Root: remove `onResult(null)` from runBacktest's start. Prior result stays visible while a new one is in flight; the Run-Backtest button's loading state is the user-facing signal.
  - Defense in depth: Optimizer + WFA panels switched to synchronous `persist(...)` wrappers around every setter (vs useEffect-on-state which depends on the component still being mounted to commit). Sensitivity gained localStorage persistence from scratch (it had none).

Branch: `main`. Frontend 238/238 tests pass. Backend 500/500 tests pass. `npm run build` clean throughout.

## 2026-05-15

### F218 — Chart perpetual repaint loop (lw-charts v4 RO pattern fighting v5 autoSize)

- User photographed naked-eye chart flicker with a phone camera — long exposure captured "ghosted" duplicated axis labels (2021/2Q21 overlapping) and smeared B/S markers on MACD/RSI sub-panes. Browser screenshots came out crisp; symptom was invisible to static capture because each frame was clean but the frame-to-frame oscillation was what blurred under the camera shutter.
- **Live measurements via chrome-devtools-mcp** on Chart tab, NVDA Daily, idle, no user input: `requestAnimationFrame` scheduled **120/sec sustained**, `getContext` called **~1320/sec**, canvas attributes mutating **120/sec** between two fixed sizes — `1298×94.375` and `1304×100.625` alternating every frame (6px width / 6.25px height — DPR fractional pixel rounding).
- Diagnosis walked through and discarded: not duplicate charts (2 canvases per container is normal lw-charts), not StrictMode (disabling it changed nothing), not crosshair ping-pong (`setCrosshairPosition` doesn't re-fire `subscribeCrosshairMove` in v5). My first fix attempt (rAF-batch the RO + skip-if-same-size) made it WORSE — rAF 240→480, mutations 240→5040 in 2s — because rAF deferral retimes the oscillator into a two-frame cycle rather than breaking it.
- Dispatched three parallel ce:review agents (`performance` + `julik-frontend-races` + `correctness`); all three confirmed at confidence ≥0.90: external `ResizeObserver` in `Chart.tsx:371` and `SubPane.tsx:172` calling `chart.applyOptions({width, height})` is a leftover v4 pattern that fights lw-charts v5's internal RO-based auto-sizing. The two never agree on the canonical canvas size due to DPR rounding → stable two-state oscillation at frame rate. `syncWidths()` from the `visibleLogicalRangeChange` handler amplifies but doesn't drive it.
- **Fix (10 lines, two files):** add `autoSize: true` to `chartOptions`, drop explicit `height` from both `createChart()` calls, delete both `new ResizeObserver(...)` blocks and their `ro.disconnect()` cleanup lines.
- **After:** rAF / getContext / canvas-mutations all = **0/sec while idle** (verified via chrome-devtools-mcp probe). User confirmed CPU usage dropped substantially in Activity Monitor. Three-pane sync, crosshair, syncWidths, regime background bands, trade markers, axis labels all intact and crisp (browser-screenshot verified on NVDA Daily with MACD>0 regime filter active).
- Postmortem with full diagnostic flow: `docs/postmortems/2026-05-15-ux-walkthrough.md` (earlier session); reviewer transcripts under `tasks/`.
- Follow-ups: **[F219]** idle-rAF canary smoke test (hooks rAF for 500ms after chart mount, asserts <5 calls — would have caught this) [testing]. **[F220]** grep remaining `new ResizeObserver` + `applyOptions({width,height})` callsites for the same v4 pattern (bot card sparklines, Discovery, equity curve overlay) [hardening].

### F215 — Idle CPU/perf bundle (5 fixes, browser-instrumented before/after)

- Trigger: user reported the frontend tab spikes CPU even when idle, drops on tab close. Used `chrome-devtools-mcp` to instrument `window.setInterval` + `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` over 10s idle windows for before/after measurement.
- **Baseline (10s idle):** 48 setInterval registrations (28 × 10000ms broker-poll observers, 20 × 5000ms tanstack timer-rearms), 7 idle API fetches, 0 long tasks. Journal payload 177 KB / fetch (542 trades).
- **Five fixes shipped together** (one impl agent, then one manual follow-up):
  1. **BotCard dropped `useBroker()`** — N×bot per-card observers each armed their own broker-poll timer. `BotControlCenter` already calls `useBroker()` so it now passes `adaptiveInterval` down as a prop. Test (`BotCard.test.tsx`) updated to pass the prop directly.
  2. **TradeJournal pairing memoized on `[trades]`** — the O(n) LIFO pairing loop was re-running on every parent re-render in BOTH TradeJournal and PositionsTable. Wrapped in `useMemo` returning `{exitPnl, exitEntryPrice}`.
  3. **Constant `refetchInterval`** in `useTradingQueries.ts` — function-form `() => adaptiveMs(...)` was re-evaluated after every fetch (clear+rearm cost); replaced with `5_000` / `30_000` constants. Removed unused `adaptiveMs` helper and `useQueryClient` calls.
  4. **`/api/trading/journal` paginated** — `routes/trading.py` `get_journal` accepts `limit: Optional[int] = None` and slices `trades[-limit:]`. Frontend: `fetchJournal` accepts `limit?`, `useJournalQuery` includes `limit` in queryKey for cache separation. `TradeJournal` defaults to `limit=200` with a `Last 100/200/500/1000/All` dropdown persisted to `strategylab-journal-limit`. CSV export does a one-shot full-history `await fetchJournal(...)` so users still get everything (button shows `…` while exporting). PositionsTable also passes `limit=200` (only needs entry timestamps for open positions).
  5. **Single-owner polling for `journal` + `bots`** (manual follow-up after first profile showed dedupe still incomplete) — removed `refetchInterval` from `useJournalQuery` + `useBotsQuery` entirely. `PaperTrading` now owns one `setInterval(5000)` calling `qc.invalidateQueries(['journal'])` + `(['bots'])`. tanstack dedupes the refetch across observers — N consumers → 1 fetch per cycle.
- **After (10s idle):** 14 setInterval registrations (4 × 5000, 6 × 10000, 4 × 30000), 9 idle fetches, 0 long tasks. Journal payload 67 KB / fetch.
- **Net deltas vs baseline:** total interval registrations **48 → 14 (−71%)**, broker-timer churn **28 → 6 (−79%)**, journal channel bandwidth **354 KB → 67 KB per 5s window (−81%)**. Long tasks remained at zero throughout.
- **Caveat surfaced during measurement:** running uvicorn had no `--reload`, so the first re-profile showed payload still at 177 KB; user restarted backend, second re-profile confirmed 67 KB. Documented because it's an easy trap when comparing builds.
- Verification: `npm run build` clean (1889 modules), `npm test -- --run` 153/153, backend `pytest tests/ -q` 485/485.
- Follow-ups: **[F216]** extend single-owner pattern to positions/orders/account for symmetry. **[F217]** "Last N of M" indicator in TradeJournal so users can see when the limit is truncating history.

### F214 — Manual close attribution (broker tag, P&L, bot card credit) + journal backfill

- User screenshot showed manual SELL rows on `BABA` / `NVDA` displaying `—` for broker, P&L, and Gain%, AND the realized P&L not contributing to the bot's total or the bot portfolio card. Bot had opened the position; user closed manually from the positions table; the journal didn't know they were related.
- **Backend:** `routes/trading.py` `/sell` now resolves `broker_name = req.broker or get_active_broker()` and looks up the owning bot via new `_find_owning_bot(symbol, direction)` helper (iterates `bot_manager.bots`, matches `cfg.symbol` + `state.entry_price is not None` + direction; regime/bidirectional bots match either side). Both the `qty=None` close-position path and the explicit-qty path pass `broker=`, `bot_id=`, `direction=` to `_log_trade`. `/buy` also tags `broker=get_active_broker()`.
- `journal.py compute_realized_pnl` extended: a row now counts when `(source == "bot")` OR `(bot_id is not None and t.bot_id == bot_id)`. Manual exits tagged with the owning `bot_id` credit the bot's realized P&L without polluting the broader "bot fills only" filter for untagged rows. Flows to bot card + portfolio card via `bot_runner._tick` recomputing on the next cycle.
- **Frontend:** `TradeJournal.tsx` pairing loop dropped the `t.source !== 'bot'` gate so manual exits pair with bot entries chronologically; `sideColor`/`reasonColor`/`rowBackground` now color manual SELL rows green/red when paired (fall back to grey when unpaired so pure-manual no-context rows stay neutral).
- **Backfill:** `bin/backfill-manual-trades.py` walks the journal chronologically, maintains a per-`(symbol, direction)` LIFO stack of unconsumed entries, and on each manual SELL/COVER (a) sets `broker="alpaca"` (the only historical broker) when missing and (b) inherits `bot_id` from the paired entry. Backs up `trade_journal.json.bak` before write. Ran `--apply` on the live journal: **8 manual rows broker-backfilled, 4 bot_id-tagged** (the other 4 had no preceding bot entry to pair with — pure-manual sequences).
- Cache safety: `journal.py` cache key is `(mtime, size)` so the in-process backend picks up the rewritten file on next read without restart.
- Verification: backend AST parse, `npm run build` clean, manual screenshot follow-up after backfill confirms ALPACA tag + green/red P&L visible on the previously-blank rows.

### F213 — Click-to-sort trades table (supersedes C26 dropdown)

- User request: replace the C26 sort dropdown with click-to-sort column headers. 12 columns sortable (`#`, Entry date/price, Exit date/price, Shares, P&L, Return, Slip, Comm, Borrow, Exit type). 3-state cycle: click → asc ↑, click again → desc ↓, third click → clear (back to chronological). Cross-column switching resets to asc on the new target. Active header rendered with `--text-primary` colour + directional arrow; inactive stay muted. Header row carries a `title` tooltip documenting the cycle.
- Comparators: a `Map(sell → index)` resolves buy-pairing once per render and feeds the slip / commission pair-sum functions; date columns reuse a `toMs()` helper that handles both ISO strings and unix-int intraday timestamps. `exit_type` ranks SL → TSL → Signal so all SL exits cluster on asc.
- Persistence: replaced the old `strategylab-trades-sort-mode` string with a `strategylab-trades-sort` JSON `{col, dir}`. Lazy `useState` initializer validates against the `SortCol` union + `SortDir` literal (no unsafe cast); `useEffect` removes the key when sort is cleared so the cleared state survives reload.
- Browser-verified end-to-end on KO RSI 30/70 daily (106 trades): asc click → most-negative-first sequence `[-1237.96, -1135.22, -678.46, -647.34, -520.17]`, desc click → biggest-wins-first `[+632.13, +574.50, +502.04, +418.29, +403.77]`, third click → chronological restored `[-8.23, +3.31, -118.36, +194.06, -13.19]` (matches baseline), cross-column click on Return → `{col:'pnl_pct', dir:'asc'}` with first 3 returns `-10.85%, -10.38%, -6.36%`. localStorage round-trip verified at each transition. Screenshot at `docs/screenshots/trades-sort-click-headers.png`.
- Verification: `npm run build` clean, frontend tests **153/153** unchanged.

### Follow-up sweep — F207 + F208 + F209 (test infra + a11y verify + sort persistence)

- **[F207]** 11 long-standing pre-existing frontend test failures triaged and fixed → **153/153 pass**. Two root causes:
  - **WalkForwardPanel 10 failures (9 async + 1 threshold).** `vi.mock('../../api/client')` was incomplete — left `api.defaults` as `undefined`, so `WalkForwardPanel`'s reference to `api.defaults.baseURL` to build the SSE fetch URL threw `TypeError` and got silently caught by the component's error handler, blocking result render. Compounding: the panel uses native `fetch()` with a `ReadableStream` SSE body, not `api.post` — the mock was patching the wrong transport. Fix: (a) add `defaults: { baseURL: '' }` to the api-client mock, (b) introduce a `makeSseFetchMock()` helper that returns a `ReadableStream` emitting `data: {"type":"result",...}\n\n` and swap `mockApiPost.mockResolvedValue` → `globalThis.fetch = makeSseFetchMock(data)` in each async test. Separate `run button disabled` test used `201` as the threshold expecting `> 200` cap; cap is actually `_MAX_COMBOS_PER_WINDOW = 1000` → bumped to `1001`.
  - **BotCard 1 failure.** Test asserted `'No activity yet.'` after clicking Show Log on a `status: 'stopped'` card, but `ActivityLog` renders `'Bot is stopped'` for that status. Updated assertion to match emitted text. No production-code change.
- **[F208]** Retry button keyboard a11y verified via `chrome-devtools-mcp`. Patched `XMLHttpRequest.prototype.send` on the live dev server to fail any `/api/indicators/` request with `readyState=4, status=0, onerror(ProgressEvent)`; clicked chart reload; React Query exhausted retries → both MACD and RSI SubPane error overlays rendered with "Failed to load: Network Error" + a focusable Retry button (`tabIndex=0`, `pointerEvents=auto`, not disabled, no overlay `pointerEvents:none`). `retryBtn.focus()` → `document.activeElement` matches; `press_key('Enter')` → 4 fresh indicator XHRs fire within 1.5s (one per subpane × 2 retries). Screenshot at `docs/screenshots/f208-retry-button.png`.
- **[F209]** C26 sort dropdown now persists via `localStorage['strategylab-trades-sort-mode']`. Lazy `useState` initializer validates the value against the literal `SortMode` union (no unsafe cast); `useEffect` writes through on every change, wrapped in try/catch for localStorage-disabled / quota-exceeded environments. Dashed key per CLAUDE.md trap note.
- **Remaining follow-ups deferred this session**: **[F211]** broker close-all under mixed long+short (needs paper-trading session); **[F212]** [next] F210 6-site inline-confirm smoke (bot-related sites need a running bot with open positions; 3 StrategyBuilder sites tractable via headless saved-strategy seeding but deferred for batch efficiency). **[F205]** parallel batch watchdog stays deferred ([hard][arch]).
- **Verification**: backend `pytest -q` 485/485, frontend `npm run build` clean, frontend `npm run test -- --run` **153/153** (was 142/153 — F207 closes the 11-failure backlog that started before this week's bundles). Atomic single commit including the test fixes, sort-persistence, screenshot evidence, and tracking updates.

### 6-item UX + reliability + infra bundle (F44/F50/F192/A14d/C26/F206 — 5 parallel agents)

- **[F206]** `routes/trading.py` direction-bias audit closed — agent found and fixed one real bug: `place_sell` qty-specified branch was submitting `BrokerOrderRequest(side="sell")` even for short positions (where the cover order should be a BUY). Now derives `order_side = "buy" if pos_is_short else "sell"` alongside the existing `cancel_side` / `log_side`. Audited 6 other `"sell"` / `_log_trade` sites and ruled them out with one-line justifications (long-only entries, broker-primitive delegations, read-only journal queries). One residual flagged as **[F211]** — `close_all_positions` delegates to broker primitive without per-symbol probe; behavior of Alpaca/IBKR close-all under mixed long+short books not directly tested.
- **[F192]** Retry button on stuck SubPane error overlay — `useInstanceIndicators` already exposed `refetch`; threaded a new `onRetryIndicators` callback prop through `App.tsx` → `Chart` → `SubPanelEntry` → `SubPane`. Removed `pointerEvents: 'none'` from the error-overlay div so the inline button is clickable, then added a small Retry button styled with existing `TEXT` / `GRID` constants (no new colors). Picked button-over-`refetchInterval` per TODO option. **[F208]** follow-up: keyboard a11y not browser-verified.
- **[A14d]** Per-pane loading map — `useInstanceIndicators` now returns `loadingByInstance: Record<string, boolean>` alongside the unchanged scalar `isLoading` (backward-compatible). Regular instances share `regularQuery.isLoading`; HTF group instances each share their group's `htfQueryResults[i].isLoading`. `Chart.tsx` `SubPanelEntry` computes per-pane loading as `group.instances.some(i => loadingByInstance[i.id])`. Only the pane(s) whose instances are actually fetching show the overlay now; previously all 3 subpanes flashed together on any HTF refetch.
- **[C26]** Trades table sort dropdown in `Results.tsx` — 5 modes (`chronological`, `pnl_high_low`, `pnl_low_high`, `hold_duration`, `exit_type`). `useMemo` keyed on `sells.length + sortMode` over a derived `sortedSells` array; buy-pairing preserved via `sells.indexOf(sell)` so entry data stays correctly paired after reorder. Hold-duration handles both string dates and unix-int intraday timestamps. **[F209]** follow-up: persist last-selected mode across reload.
- **[F44]** Replaced 6 `alert()` / `confirm()` calls with inline confirmation rows / error spans, mirroring the `FundBar` pattern. Sites:

  - `BotCard.tsx:313` menu Reset P&L + `BotCard.tsx:628` expanded-card Reset P&L → shared `confirmingResetPnl` state (with `e.stopPropagation()` to prevent expand toggle)
  - `StrategyBuilder.tsx:49` commission-migration alert → dismissable `migrationNotice` banner
  - `StrategyBuilder.tsx:272` rename-conflict alert → `renameError` inline error span; clears on keystroke
  - `StrategyBuilder.tsx:320` import-replace confirm → `importConfirm` inline strip with deferred `commitImport()`
  No global Toast/Modal abstraction introduced — each site keeps local state. **[F210]** follow-up: 6 sites not browser-verified yet.

Documented in `docs/overnight-builder-prompt-patch.md` as new §3.5b. Local run: clean exit 0, port 4173 freed.

### 10-item easy hardening bundle (orchestrator workflow, Tier A — 6 parallel agents)

- **[F200]** Per-symbol watchdog inside F127 batch deadline — `_run_quick` now invoked via `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1).submit(...).result(timeout=remaining)` where `remaining = max(0, deadline - time.monotonic())`. On `concurrent.futures.TimeoutError` the symbol gets `error="quick-backtest timed out"` + `error_code="timed_out"` and `deadline_hit=True` flips so the tail short-circuits to `error="deadline exceeded"`. Test: 0.5s sleep + 0.1s budget asserts first symbol times out, second short-circuits.
- **[F201]** First-symbol deadline-at-entry contract — docstring on `quick_backtest_batch` now states "If the configured budget has already elapsed before the first symbol is reached, all symbols short-circuit to error='deadline exceeded' with no `_run_quick` invocations." Option (a) from the TODO — under-specification fix, no behavioral change.
- **[F202]** `error_code` Literal companion field — added `error_code: Optional[Literal["deadline_exceeded", "timed_out", "no_data", "internal"]] = None` to `QuickBacktestResult`. Four module constants (`BATCH_ERROR_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED`, `BATCH_ERROR_TIMED_OUT`, `BATCH_ERROR_NO_DATA_PREFIX`, `BATCH_ERROR_INTERNAL`) single-source the free-text sentinels. `error_code` set on every error path in `_run_quick` + `quick_backtest_batch`. Free-text `error` field unchanged (additive).
- **[F203]** `.env.example` — appended commented entries for `STRATEGYLAB_BATCH_DEADLINE_SECS=30` and `STRATEGYLAB_MAX_BYTES=1048576` so operators and agents reading OpenAPI can discover the env names. Doc-only.
- **[F191]** `apiErrorDetail` — after the `Array.isArray(detail)` branch, now handles non-array object detail: prefers `detail.reason` then `detail.error` (both guarded by `typeof x === 'string' && x`). Closes the F98 structured `{error, reason}` case where the helper was falling through to the generic Axios message.
- **[F193]** Wfa_pool serial-vs-serial test — docstring on `test_biased_partial_drop_equivalence_serial_vs_serial` now explicitly marks the coverage gap: parallel subprocess isolation prevents biased_partial injection; the parallel path's drop logic is covered structurally by F173's shared `_run_window_core` helper. End-to-end synthetic test with extremely short timeout filed as follow-up.
