### F173 + F189 + F190 architecture refactor bundle (orchestrator workflow, Tier B+)

- **F173** `wfa_pool.py` cleanup — three parts shipped: (a) precise type annotations on `run_windows_parallel` and `_run_windows_serial`; (b) `_WorkerArgs(NamedTuple)` replaces the 11-element positional worker-args tuple; (c) `_run_window_core` helper extracts ~155 lines of IS-winner-selection + stability-tagging + OOS-evaluation that were duplicated near-verbatim across the multiprocessing worker and the serial path. The duplication is what produced F166 C-01 P0 (biased-partial drop logic in only one path) — that class of bug is now structurally impossible. Helper takes all callables positionally (`run_grid_fn`, `run_backtest_fn`, `combo_key_fn`, `get_neighbor_keys_fn`, `apply_param_fn`) so the spawn-context worker can resolve them locally without pickling closures. Net wfa_pool.py: 610 → 593 lines.
- **F189** `requestSignature` write/read asymmetry — extracted typed `CacheKey` interface (17 fields, each typed via `StrategyRequest['ticker']`-style lookups) + `cacheWriteKey(req): CacheKey` projection; `requestSignature(req): string` now defined as `JSON.stringify(cacheWriteKey(req))`. Single source of truth for the field list. 7 new unit tests in `requestSignature.test.ts`.
- **F190** `BacktestSummary` consolidated to `shared/types/strategy.ts`; `BotSummary.backtest_summary` retyped from `Record<string, number> | null` to `BacktestSummary | null`; F181's `Record<string, number | null>` cast removed from `BotCard.tsx`.
- **Review tier B+ (3 personas, parallel — correctness, kieran-typescript, adversarial)** — picked adversarial specifically because wfa_pool already produced a P0; warranted by risk receipt, not item count.
  - **P0 correctness C-01 (0.88)** — F189 initial implementation persisted `cacheWriteKey(lastRequest)` instead of the full request. The cache is re-read into `lastRequest` state and passed to `WalkForwardPanel` / `OptimizerPanel` as the backend request body — stripping it dropped `initial_capital`, `position_size`, `source`, `slippage_bps`, `per_share_rate`, `min_per_order`, `borrow_rate_annual`, `stop_loss`, `trailing_stop`, `dynamic_sizing`, `skip_after_stop`, `trading_hours`, `direction` after every page reload, silently substituting Pydantic defaults. Fix: write side stores the full request; only the comparison predicate uses the projection.
  - **P1 kieran-typescript K1 (0.88)** — F189 initial impl widened `requestSignature` to accept `Record<string, unknown>` to support both shapes, defeating the structural-enforcement goal. Fixed by typing `cacheWriteKey` to return a named `CacheKey` interface and having `requestSignature` call it internally — no widening needed.
  - **P2 correctness C-02 / adversarial ADV-1 (0.82)** — F173 serial path replaced original `continue` with literal `break` after a timed-out non-biased-partial window. Functionally equivalent given run_grid's clock alignment with the outer loop, but defensive-mirroring of the original is preferred. Collapsed the three is_timed_out branches (biased_partial / zero combos / full grid at deadline) into a single `continue`-style path; the loop-top deadline check remains the canonical termination signal.
  - **Debunked**: adversarial ADV-2 (`skipped_for_no_is_trades` semantics) — original always set the flag when `len==0` regardless of `is_timed_out`; helper preserves that exactly. Pre-existing 11 frontend test failures (`WalkForwardPanel.test.tsx` baseURL infra) confirmed pre-existing on clean main, not caused by this bundle.

### F198 — IBKR close orders auto-cancelled due to NYSE-direct routing (root-caused via new debug surface)

- Follow-on to F195. After the routing fix, manual IBKR closes still silently failed — `close_position` returned `fill_price=null, fill_qty=null` and `/api/trading/orders` showed the order as `cancelled` with no submitted timestamp.
- Added a 50-entry ring-buffer `_recent_errors` on `IBKRTradingProvider` (hooked from existing `_on_ib_error`) and a `GET /api/debug/ibkr-errors` endpoint. Triggering the close once captured the full ib_insync error chain — **code 10311** ("This order will be directly routed to NYSE. Direct routed orders may result in higher trade fees. Restriction is specified in Precautionary Settings of Global Configuration/API.") followed by **code 201** ("Order rejected - reason:Order was discarded.").
- **Root cause**: `close_position` placed orders against `p.contract` (the position's stored contract) — IBKR records the position's `contract.exchange` as the actual fill exchange ("NYSE"), not "SMART". Gateway's Precautionary Settings → "Bypass direct routed orders for API" refuse direct-routed orders by default, so IBKR discards the order.
- **Fix**: rebuild the contract via `self._contract(symbol)` (= `Stock(symbol, "SMART", "USD")`) for both `close_position` and `close_all_positions`. The contract used for `placeOrder` is now always SMART-routed regardless of where the position originally filled.
- Diagnostic endpoint kept — useful for future IBKR investigations where backend stderr isn't reachable from the agent session. Tradeoff: it's a debug surface with no auth gate; acceptable on localhost paper-trading scope, would need protecting if backend were ever exposed.
- User closed the live IBKR BABA position via **IBKR Mobile** in parallel with the fix work — the SMART-route patch is verified mechanically but the API end-to-end retry would require a uvicorn full restart (the `broker.py` auto-reload de-registered the IBKR provider; lifespan startup re-registers on a clean restart, not on `--reload`-triggered module reload).

### F195 — Close button routed to wrong broker (live incident fix)

- User clicked Close on an IBKR BABA LONG 7 position; nothing happened in the UI but an unrelated Alpaca BABA SHORT 30 got bought-to-cover. Root cause: `placeSell()` never sent a `broker` field and `/api/trading/sell` called `get_trading_provider()` with no args, defaulting to the global `_active_broker` (`"alpaca"`). All cross-broker manual closes were silently mis-routed.
- Fix is small and contained: **(1)** `frontend/src/api/trading.ts` — `placeSell(symbol, qty?, broker?)`; **(2)** `frontend/src/features/trading/PositionsTable.tsx` — handler takes `(symbol, broker, side)`, passes broker to `placeSell`, keys the `closing` state by `${symbol}|${broker}|${side}` so the same symbol on two brokers no longer disables both rows together; **(3)** `backend/routes/trading.py` — `SellRequest.broker: Optional[str]`, `provider = get_trading_provider(req.broker)`.
- Verified via curl: `POST /api/trading/sell {"symbol":"ZZZZNOPE","broker":"ibkr"}` returns IBKR's 404 ("No open position for ZZZZNOPE") and the global active broker stays `"alpaca"` — no global side-effect from a per-request broker override. `npm run build` clean.
- **Three follow-ups surfaced** and added to TODO.md: **[F196]** `_log_trade` in the manual /sell route still hardcodes `"sell"` (mislabels cover-shorts); **[F197]** pre-close stop-cancel cleanup hardcodes side="sell" (wrong-sided on shorts); **[F198]** the underlying IBKR auto-cancellation of the actual close order (orders 15 + 17, mid-RTH, both cancelled within 2s with no visible reason) — backend stderr isn't reachable from the orchestrator session, so this needs either a uvicorn-stderr-to-file redirect or a small in-process ring buffer of IBKR errorEvent payloads exposed via a debug endpoint. The user closed BABA on IBKR manually via IB Gateway as the immediate workaround.

### B35 stale-closure — real-browser verification + ref-mirror fix (interactive)

- Live-Chrome regression test of the 20-item bundle's B35 caught that the holistic fixer's "close over `updated`" patch did NOT actually fix the bug — the snapshot is captured at delete time, so a concurrent save during the 5s undo window is still silently overwritten when the timer expires.
- Browser flow: chrome-devtools-mcp + seeded localStorage (`strategylab-saved-strategies` ← [StratA, StratB, StratC]) → select StratA via native `change` event → click Delete → assert localStorage stays `[A, B, C]` for the full 5s window → at t≈6s localStorage = `[B, C]` confirming the timer's persist call fires.
- Fix: `savedStrategiesRef` mirrors `savedStrategies` via `useEffect`; the `setInterval` finalize step now calls `persistSavedStrategies(savedStrategiesRef.current)` instead of the stale `updated` array, so any concurrent save/rename/pin during the undo window survives the timer expiry. Removed the stale `updated` closure capture; kept `name` and the deps array (`[savedStrategies, activeStrategyName]`).
- Side-find filed as **[F185]**: `loadSavedStrategy(s)` blanks the page with `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'enabled')` if a saved strategy lacks `tradingHours` — `setTradingHours(s.tradingHours)` has no fallback. Caught while seeding browser-test fixtures; the same shape risk applies to `dynamicSizing`/`skipAfterStop` for legacy localStorage rows.
- Process note: the holistic fixer's "snapshot-the-array" fix was technically minimal but assumed the snapshot would still be representative of latest state at timer expiry — which is exactly the property the bug was about. Static reasoning had marked it OK; the live-browser test surfaced the residual hole in <2 minutes. Cementing real-loop verification as the right tool for setInterval-style state-divergence bugs (the JSDOM/RTL fake-timers path would have passed this).

### 20-item Tier B bundle — A14b, B34, B35, D27a, F59, F61, F62, F71, F74, F83, F84, F89, F105, F146, F154, F160, F164, F167, F168, F174 (interactive, full orchestrator workflow)

- User requested 20 picks with full orchestrator workflow. Routing: 5 parallel Sonnet implementers (frontend UX, frontend hardening, backend atomic-writes, backend mixed, backend tests). Total bundle ~510 insertions / 262 deletions across 25+ files.
- **Frontend UX** (Agent A: A14b, B34, B35, D27a): React Query `isFetching` exposed in `useOHLCV` so chart skeleton shows on stale-cache refetches. B35 replaced `confirm()` with a portaled 5s undo toast — optimistic UI remove + persist-on-expiry. B34's "fat-finger separator" became minimal once B35 made deletion reversible — just spacer + muted color. D27a's `StatusBadge` replaces native `title=` tooltips with a positioned-div popover (mirrors `TradeTooltip.tsx`).
- **Frontend hardening** (Agent B: F154, F160): F154 audit found that only `StrategyComparison.tsx` needed the F152 stale-snapshot fix — `BotCard` reads localStorage inline at render (fresh), `StrategyBuilder` is the writer (always current), `AddBotBar` was already correct. F160 mirrored F175's WFA `AbortController` pattern into `OptimizerPanel` + `SensitivityPanel` (`abortControllerRef`, abort-on-submit, `signal:` in request, ignore `AbortError`/`ERR_CANCELED`, abort on unmount).
- **Backend atomic-writes** (Agent C: F71+F74+F83): new `backend/fileutil.py::atomic_write_text(path, content)` helper — same-directory NamedTemporaryFile + `fd.flush()` + `os.fsync(fd.fileno())` + try/except close + `os.replace` + best-effort temp cleanup. 4 sites migrated (journal.py, bot_manager.py, routes/trading.py, routes/providers.py). One behavior change in `routes/providers.py`: previously `shutil.copymode()` + conditional `chmod 0600`; now unconditional `chmod 0600` post-rename (strictly safer for a secrets file). 5 unit tests in `tests/test_fileutil.py`.
- **Backend mixed** (Agent D: F59, F62, F105, F174): F62 added `_journal_cache` + `_journal_cache_key = (mtime, size)` tuple (size avoids the 1-second-mtime-granularity collision). F59 surfaces `_log_trade` failures via `state.error_message` + `asyncio.create_task(notify_error)`. F105 added `Interval = Literal[...]` across 6 Pydantic surfaces (`StrategyRequest`, `BotConfig`, `QuickBacktestRequest`, `BatchQuickBacktestRequest`, `IndicatorsPostRequest`, `get_ohlcv` query param). F174 audited 9 files for module-level side effects, annotated with `# F174: WORKER-SAFE: <reason>` comments — no genuinely unsafe statements found.
- **Backend tests** (Agent E: F61, F84, F89, F146, F164, F167, F168): F167 extracted `_patch_run_backtest(monkeypatch, mock_fn)` helper in `test_walk_forward.py`, used at 18 dual-patch sites. F164 tightened ~26 `mock_run_backtest` signatures (`**kwargs` → explicit kwarg list). F89 converted TestClient fixtures to module-scope context-manager form (function-scope made suite 32× slower; module-scope reproduces pre-F89 module-level constant semantics + adds lifespan shutdown). F61 new `test_exits.py` (6 stop-loss + trailing-stop tests). F84 new BotCard test for `state: undefined` path. F146 6 new `BotManager.load()` residual coverage tests. F168 new `test_grid_runner.py` (7 direct unit tests).
- **Review tier B (4 P1 + 4 P2)**: 3 parallel personas — correctness + kieran-python + kieran-typescript — produced converging findings on B35's stale-closure bug.
  - **P1 (caught + fixed)**: **B35 stale-closure (KT-2 + C-01 converged)** — `setInterval` callback re-filtered `savedStrategies` from the render closure. If user saved a strategy DURING the 5s undo window, the new strategy was silently erased from localStorage on countdown expiry. UI showed the saved strategy fine; only the persisted store diverged. Fix: close over the already-computed `updated` snapshot. **B35 timer cleanup (KT-1)** — ref typed `setTimeout`, used as `setInterval`, no unmount cleanup → setState-after-unmount risk. **F62 mutable cache (KP-1)** — `_load_trades` returned the internal list. Defensive `list(_journal_cache)` copy. **F62 mtime granularity (KP-2)** — replaced single mtime with `(mtime, size)` tuple. **F62 invalidation ordering (kieran-python residual)** — `_log_trade` now nulls cache BEFORE `atomic_write_text` so a raised write still invalidates.
  - **P2 (fixed)**: **C-04 F167 incomplete** — 5 orphan dual-patch sites including one (test_intraday_5m_window_boundaries) that patched ONLY `wf_mod.run_backtest`, leaving `grid_runner_mod` to potentially hit the real backtest. Holistic fixer migrated all 5. **KT-4** `catch (e: any)` in F160's panels → `catch (e: unknown)` with narrow `{ name?, code? }` cast. **KT-5** `as never` in F84 test → proper `minimalConfig: BotConfig` fixture. **KP-5** `test_grid_runner` test #7 misnamed → `test_run_grid_single_combo` with trimmed docstring.
  - **Deferred to follow-ups (F181-F184)**: `BotCard.tsx as any` on `backtest_summary` (preexisting), exits.py untyped public-surface methods (preexisting), F174 `_data_rate_counter` comment over-explains (cosmetic), F105 `"60m"` accepted by Pydantic but rejected by IBKR provider (needs design decision).
- **Verification**: 461 backend pytest pass (was 433; +28 new tests — F61 6, F84 1, F89 fixture conversion, F146 6, F168 7, test_fileutil 5, test_journal_cache 4). `npm run build` clean. Suite went 2.5s → 19s due to F89's module-scoped TestClient lifespan startups (8s for two `client` fixtures × 2 test files). Acceptable.
- **Process notes**:
  - **Pattern that earned its keep again**: holistic fixer over per-finding fixers. B35's stale-closure (KT-2) and timer-cleanup (KT-1) both touched the same `deleteWithUndo` function; F62's three findings (mutable list, mtime granularity, invalidation ordering) all touched `journal.py` cache logic. One fixer pass kept the changes coherent.
  - **Two reviewers converging on the same P1 from different angles** (KT-2 + C-01 on B35 stale-closure) was the strongest signal — both took different routes to the same bug. Worth tracking as evidence that 3-persona Tier B has real value at this batch size.
  - F89 function-scope vs module-scope: orchestrator had to swap after the agent picked function-scope (suite went 80s). The brief had warned but agent picked the safer-looking option. Module-scope reproduces the pre-F89 module-level constant semantics; suite back to 19s.

"No activity yet." becomes "Waiting for next tick" (running) or "Bot is stopped" (stopped); default fallback unchanged. Both call sites (lines 302, 601) pass `status={summary.status}`.

### 10-item Tier A polish bundle — A15, B30, B32, B33, B36, C27, D28, D29, F55, F157 (interactive)

- User asked for 10 Tier A/B items via orchestrator workflow. All 10 picks were `[easy]` (Tier A — no persona review), grouped by file independence for 4 parallel Sonnet implementer dispatches: Results.tsx (A15+B36+C27), StrategyBuilder.tsx (B32+B33), RuleRow+AddBotBar (B30+D28+D29), backend (F55+F157). Total bundle 64 insertions / 18 deletions across 6 files — well within Tier A's <100-line ceiling, no contract surface change.
- **A15** — Results.tsx:625 chart container flipped `overflow: 'hidden'` → `'auto'` so the existing `resize: 'vertical'` handle renders. Two mutually-exclusive CSS properties were the entire bug.
- **B30** — RuleRow.tsx:526 ruleRow style adds `overflowX: 'auto'` + `flexWrap: 'nowrap'`. Browser only renders the bar when content overflows, so simple MA/RSI rows unaffected; complex Stochastic K/D/Smooth rows scroll horizontally instead of clipping.
- **B32** — StrategyBuilder.tsx:346-362 window `keydown` listener fires `runBacktest()` on Cmd/Ctrl+Enter. Guards skip when activeElement is a `<textarea>` or `contentEditable`, `preventDefault()` for the chord. **Orchestrator caught a stale-closure bug in the Sonnet implementer's first cut:** empty-deps `useEffect` with a direct `runBacktest()` call would have captured the mount-time closure, so every Cmd+Enter would read mount-time `buyRules`/`sellRules` and ignore live edits. Fix: `runBacktestRef = useRef<() => void>()` updated on every render (no-deps effect), listener invokes `runBacktestRef.current()` instead — listener stays bound once, closure stays fresh. Standard pattern for "register once, dispatch latest."
- **B33** — StrategyBuilder.tsx: 13px white border-spinner (CSS `@keyframes sb-spin`, no external lib) replaces the Play icon while loading; button spreads `opacity: 0.6` + `cursor: 'not-allowed'`. Inline `<style>` injected once at the top of the return — match the file's existing inline-style idiom.
- **B36** — Results.tsx:391 outer tabBar wrapper switched `flexWrap: 'wrap'` → `'nowrap'` + added `overflowX: 'auto'`; inner flex row pinned to `'nowrap'`; individual tab buttons get `whiteSpace: 'nowrap'` + `flexShrink: 0`. 10+ tabs now scroll horizontally on narrow panes instead of stacking unpredictably.
- **C27** — Results.tsx:484-519 split the single `(summary || equity)` metrics block into two branches. Summary keeps the full 8-metric strip; Equity now renders a 2-metric compact header (Total Return % + Sharpe) with `padding: '8px 16px'` (vs 12px). Same visual language, saves ~60px vertical space on the Equity tab.
- **D28** — AddBotBar.tsx:43,89,95,135,240-243 adds `adding` state. `handleAdd` early-returns if already adding, sets the flag before the async POST, resets in `finally`. Submit button disabled while adding; label flips to "Adding…". Rapid clicks no longer create duplicate bots.
- **D29** — AddBotBar.tsx:81 `onStrategyChange` also calls `setDirection((s.direction as 'long' | 'short') ?? 'long')` alongside the existing symbol/interval copies. Selecting a short strategy in the dropdown no longer silently leaves the new bot at long — eliminates the wrong-way-trade risk.
- **F55** — bot_runner.py: 7 `self.manager.save()` call sites inside `async def` functions (lines 198, 335, 506, 529, 534, 545, 552) wrapped with `await asyncio.to_thread(...)`. Line 468 (`_on_ibkr_error`, sync callback from IBKR's EReader thread) intentionally left as direct `self.manager.save()` — same "no running event loop on this thread" pattern documented in CLAUDE.md's Key Bugs Fixed (`run_coroutine_threadsafe` rule). 431/431 pytest unchanged.
- **F157** — routes/backtest.py:358 comment rewritten to clarify that `all_rules` already holds the unified buy+sell pool, the b23 branch appends direction-specific rules for indicator pre-computation, and duplicates are harmless because `compute_indicators` caches by `(indicator, params)`. Pure prose; no code change.
- **Tier A verification protocol:** orchestrator did `npm run build` + full `pytest -x -q` (431/431) + grep-spot-checked all 6 files. No persona review. The B32 stale-closure catch validates the verification step — Tier A's "no review" only works if the orchestrator actually reads the changes. **Pattern note:** parallel Sonnet implementers occasionally ship subtly stale-closure-prone React effects when the prompt asks for a `useEffect` with empty deps but doesn't explicitly mention closure freshness. Worth flagging in future prompts for similar tasks.

### F176 + F177 + F178 + F179 — F175 test follow-ups (interactive, Tier B)

- Four testing gaps logged during F175's review pass, all cleared in one bundle. User asked for "full orchestration workflow" — Opus orchestrator + haiku explore + 2 parallel Sonnet implementers (backend bundle + frontend extract+test) + 3-persona parallel review + Opus synthesis + 1 holistic Sonnet fixer pass.
- **[F176](TODO-archive.md#f176)** — Parallel-path progress test. Lives in NEW `backend/tests/test_wfa_pool.py` (intentionally separate from `test_walk_forward.py` because the latter's autouse `_force_serial_wfa` fixture would defeat the test). Uses real ProcessPool dispatch (`n_windows == _MIN_WINDOWS_FOR_POOL == 4` guarantees the parallel path), synthetic 170-bar df + trivial RSI rules so workers complete in ~1.2s. Assertion set covers callback fires exactly `n_windows` times, completed values cover `{1..n}`, max call is `(n, n)`, results dict full, `timed_out=False`. Review (correctness) caught an architectural gap: no assertion that `_FORCE_SERIAL == False` at test entry, so a leaked fixture from another module would silently run serial path and pass all assertions. Fixed with `assert not wfa_pool_mod._FORCE_SERIAL` guard at the top — leaked fixtures now fail loudly.
- **[F177](TODO-archive.md#f177)** — Cancel-signal integration test. The over-narrow-monkeypatch trap: F175's cancel_event is constructed by `wf_mod.threading.Event()`, so monkeypatching `threading.Event` mutates the global module attribute — but the initial assertion `len(set_calls) >= 1` then passes if ANY Event in the process fires `set()`, not specifically the cancel_event. Correctness review caught this as P0. Fix: subclass `threading.Event` into `_RecordingEvent` that captures every constructed instance in a `instances: list`. Assertion becomes `instances[-1].is_set() == True` — the LAST-constructed Event (= the cancel_event inside `event_stream()`) was specifically set. Subclassing rather than wrapping also resolved kieran-python K4 (isinstance compatibility) in the same fix. Mock signature also tightened to `_stub_run_backtest(req, *, include_spy_correlation=True, indicator_cache=None, df=None)` so a new production kwarg surfaces as TypeError instead of being swallowed by `**kw`.
- **[F178](TODO-archive.md#f178)** — NaN-in-metrics serialisation test. Monkeypatches `wf_mod._assemble_walk_forward_response` to inject `wfe=float('nan')` into an otherwise-valid response, asserts the stream emits `started` → `error` (status=500, detail mentions "serialis") with no `result` event. Pins the `json.dumps(allow_nan=False)` → error-event path added by F175. Solid as-shipped per all three reviewers; mock signature tightened alongside F177.
- **[F179](TODO-archive.md#f179)** — Frontend SSE malformed-event test. Required minimal refactor: extracted the inline `JSON.parse + try/catch` from `WalkForwardPanel.tsx` into `frontend/src/features/strategy/sseParser.ts` (24-line pure function `parseSseFrame(dataLines, label='SSE event'): unknown | null`). Both call sites in the panel (main read loop + flush loop) now call the helper; flush site passes `label='SSE event in flush'` to preserve the diagnostic distinction the inline code had. Test file co-located per project convention (`sseParser.test.ts` next to source, not in central `src/test/`) — kieran-typescript review K4 caught the wrong location. 7 test cases: valid event, multi-line event, empty input (silent null), malformed JSON (warn + null), truncated JSON (warn + null), JSON `null` literal (silent null — not malformed), whitespace-only line (warn + null). kieran-typescript also caught a real P1 in the panel: `as SseEvent | null` is a compile-time cast, not runtime — a non-null non-SseEvent JSON object (e.g. `{"foo": "bar"}`) would pass the null guard and silently fall through every branch of the type switch. Fix: added `typeof evt.type !== 'string'` discriminant guard at both call sites; non-conforming events now log+skip.
- **Review tier B (1 P0 + 4 P1 + 4 P2):** Three parallel reviewers (correctness + kieran-python + kieran-typescript — skipped reliability and adversarial because the diff is pure test additions, no production logic change, no contract surface). Findings interconnected enough for one holistic fixer pass. P0 + 4 P1 + 4 P2 all applied; one P2 (K1 — register `@pytest.mark.slow` in pytest.ini, drop the one-off `SKIP_SLOW_TESTS` env var) deferred as **[F180](TODO-archive.md#f180)** — works correctly today, convention cleanup. 431/431 backend pytest. 7/7 frontend vitest on sseParser. `npm run build` clean. Not visually verified (test code only).
- **Pattern note:** the test-extract refactor (F179 inline-to-helper) is a worthwhile precedent. Inline `try/catch` blocks in hot async loops are testable only by mounting the surrounding component; extracting to a pure function pays for itself the moment any test is needed. The 24-line helper has zero runtime cost over the inline code.

### F175 — WFA SSE streaming + cap raise + Tier C review fixes (interactive, arch)

- User had asked for real progress (F171's time-based bar was synthetic; "every finished task should move the progress bar"). F175 added a real SSE endpoint that ships `started` / `progress` / `result` / `error` events as windows finish, swapping the frontend's progress bar over to event-driven. Same session also lifted the F162/F163/F166/F169 speedups into a higher cap: `_MAX_COMBOS_PER_WINDOW` 200 → 1000 (WFA's indicator cache + window-level parallelism amortize cost so larger grids are reasonable; the timeout is the real safety net), `_WFA_TIMEOUT_SECS` 120 → 600.
- **[F175](TODO-archive.md#f175)** Backend: new `POST /api/backtest/walk_forward/stream` route. Shared `_setup_walk_forward(req) → WalkForwardSetup` (NamedTuple) and `_assemble_walk_forward_response(...)` extracted so the sync + stream endpoints share validation, fetch, window construction, and post-processing. Stream route runs setup via `await asyncio.to_thread(_setup_walk_forward, req)` so the event loop is never blocked during `_fetch`. Threading: worker thread bridges to async via `asyncio.Queue` + `loop.call_soon_threadsafe`; each `call_soon_threadsafe` call wrapped in `try/except RuntimeError: pass` so a client-side disconnect (loop closed) doesn't silently kill the worker thread. `threading.Event` cancel signal set by the async generator's `finally` lets the progress callback short-circuit once the client is gone. All 5 SSE payloads use `json.dumps(allow_nan=False)`; NaN/Inf in metrics emits an `error` event instead of leaking invalid JSON. `run_windows_parallel` switched from `wait()` to `as_completed()` so windows report as they finish; added `progress_callback: Optional[Callable[[int, int], None]]` kwarg with default `None` to preserve serial-path behavior.
- **Frontend:** `WalkForwardPanel` now opens `fetch()` against the stream endpoint with a `ReadableStream` reader + `TextDecoder`, parses `data:`-prefixed JSON events, drives a real `setProgress({completed, total})` per progress event, snaps to 100% on result, surfaces error events. `SseEvent` discriminated union types the parser. `AbortController` aborts the stream on unmount or Cancel button. `reader.releaseLock()` in finally. `JSON.parse` wrapped in try/catch (logs to `console.warn`, doesn't propagate). Decoder final-flush handled after the read loop ends. Cap raise + amber warning at 500+ combos.
- **Orchestrator workflow (Tier C):** Opus orchestrator + Sonnet implementer → orchestrator verify (428/428 pytest + real WFA stream end-to-end) → 4-persona parallel review (correctness + kieran-python + reliability + kieran-typescript) → orchestrator synthesize → **one holistic Sonnet fixer pass** applied 2 P0 + 10 P1 + 3 P2 fixes coherently. Fixes were interconnected (REL-001 cancel signal + REL-002 try/except share the same cleanup story; KP-01 NamedTuple cascades to both call sites; KT-2 AbortController pairs with the timer hook). Per-finding fixers would have collided. P0+P1 fixes verbatim from synthesis: REL-001 (cancel_event), REL-002 (call_soon_threadsafe try/except across 5 sites), REL-003/KP-02 (allow_nan=False on all SSE payloads), KP-01 (`WalkForwardSetup` NamedTuple replacing 6-tuple unpack at both call sites), KP-03 (thread-safety comment), KP-04 (`-> StreamingResponse` annotation), C6 (`asyncio.to_thread` for setup), KT-1 (SseEvent discriminated union), KT-2 (AbortController + Cancel button), KT-3/KP-07 (releaseLock + try/catch), C4/KT-4 (decoder flush + multi-event-per-chunk parsing), KP-05 (`Callable` from `collections.abc`), KP-06 (test docstring), KT-5 (progress snap to 100% on result).
- **Three real-world bugs caught after the fixer ran (none surfaced by reviewers, all caught by the user testing it):**
  - **Frontend was hiding the actual backend error.** `apiErrorDetail({ response: { data: rawTextString }, ... })` — `data` was a raw string from `await resp.text()`, so `data.detail` was always undefined and the user only saw the generic fallback "Walk-forward failed". Fixed in `WalkForwardPanel.tsx:620-625` (parse the body as JSON before passing in) + `errors.ts` (handle FastAPI's array-form 422 `detail`).
  - **Relative URL broke SSE in dev mode.** Every other API call goes through `axios` with `VITE_API_URL || 'http://localhost:8000'` baked in. F175's `fetch('/api/backtest/walk_forward/stream')` used a relative URL — fine in production where uvicorn serves both, broken in dev where the frontend is on `:5173` and there's no vite proxy. Result: HTTP 404 the moment the user clicked Run. Fix: import the axios client and prefix the URL with `api.defaults.baseURL`. Generalizable lesson: any time an SSE endpoint can't go through axios, copy the resolved base URL.
  - **Uvicorn was running stale code.** PID 3021 was started at 8:01PM without `--reload`, so the fixer's allow_nan / cancel_event / asyncio.to_thread edits weren't loaded. Identified via lsof + PID start time; resolved by user restart. Not a code bug, just a working-environment trap.
- **Performance arc.** Full chain F162 → F163 → F166 → F169 → F171 → F175 took the user's NVDA 5m 1y WFA from 1m55s → ~18s with live per-window progress events. F175's headline UX delta: bar moves with real completion, not synthetic time. 428/428 pytest. `npm run build` clean. Visually verified by user ("works"). Added 4 follow-up test items ([F176](TODO-archive.md#f176)-[F179](TODO-archive.md#f179)) for the parallel-path / cancel-signal / NaN / frontend-malformed-event gaps.

### F171 — Run-progress feedback for WFA / Optimizer / Sensitivity (interactive, polish — two-pass)

- After F166 dropped WFA wall-clock to ~18s, user noted the frozen button during the wait was the new biggest UX gap. First pass shipped a `useElapsedSeconds(active)` hook that ticked every 250ms while `active=true`. Second pass (same session) extended it to capture the final duration + added a progress bar — user feedback: "we should have a final time at the end as well. And a progress bar."
- **[F171](TODO-archive.md#f171)** Final shape: shared `useRequestTimer(active)` hook in `frontend/src/shared/hooks/useRequestTimer.ts` returns `{elapsed, final}`. `elapsed` ticks during the run; on the falling edge, the cleanup callback captures `final` as the total elapsed seconds and keeps it stable until the next run resets it. Wired into all three strategy panels. WFA gets the most-treatment: (1) live `Elapsed: 12s / ~6m 27s estimated · 440 backtests` banner during loading; (2) thin time-based progress bar (synthetic — `width = min(99%, elapsed / estimatedSeconds * 100%)`, capped at 99% so the user knows it isn't done until the result lands); (3) post-run `Completed in 18s · 440 backtests (96% under estimate)` calibration callout that compares actual vs estimated (the user gets both feedback on the run AND data to tune their expectations next time). Optimizer + Sensitivity get the simpler treatment: live elapsed in button text + post-run `Completed in Ns` next to the button.
- **Implementation note:** progress bar is purely time-based because backend doesn't stream progress events (no SSE, no per-window callback). True progress would need either websocket streaming or a polling endpoint. Time-based is honest enough — the divisor is the same estimator that already produces the pre-flight number, so over/under-estimates are visible the same way to the user during the run as after it. If real streaming becomes worth it (e.g., for very long runs where the estimate is wildly off), reconsider.
- Hook uses `performance.now()` deltas instead of an accumulating counter so wall-clock stays honest under browser tab throttling. File renamed mid-session from `useElapsedSeconds.ts` → `useRequestTimer.ts` to reflect the broader contract. `npm run build` clean. Not visually verified.

### F166 — Window-level parallel WFA (interactive, Tier C)

- After F169 dropped the NVDA 5m 1y WFA to 36s in-process (57s real-world), user OK'd the parallel design. F163 had prototyped IS-grid-level parallelism and lost to serial-with-cache; F166 moves the parallelism boundary to the WINDOW level. Each worker owns one complete window (IS grid + OOS backtest), so spawn cost amortizes over ~50 backtests of real work instead of fighting against the 250ms IS grid.
- **[F166](TODO-archive.md#f166)** New `backend/routes/wfa_pool.py`: per-request `ProcessPoolExecutor` with `mp.get_context("spawn")`, sized to `min(cpu_count, n_windows)`. Pool initializer ships `pickle.dumps(full_df)` once per worker process via `initargs`; `_init_worker` deserializes into a module-global `_WORKER_DF` that every per-window call slices in-process. Workers return `WindowOutput` NamedTuples; main route collects them into `dict[int, WindowOutput]` so out-of-order completion is handled cleanly, then iterates `windows` in index order to compute `scale_factor` against `prev_final_equity` and stitch the OOS equity curve. Serial fallback when `n < _MIN_WINDOWS_FOR_POOL` (=4, empirical break-even) OR `_FORCE_SERIAL=True` (test seam — subprocess workers can't see monkeypatches; autouse fixture in `tests/test_walk_forward.py` forces serial for all 41 tests).
- **Orchestrator workflow (Tier C):** Opus orchestrator + Sonnet implementer → orchestrator verify (425/425 pytest + real WFA timing 14.97s in-process) → parallel 3-persona review (correctness + kieran-python + reliability — downgraded from 5 because the diff was Python-only and concurrency-bounded) → orchestrator synthesize → **one holistic Sonnet fixer pass** applied 9 mechanical fixes across 3 files in a coherent sweep (lifecycle, error handling, biased-partial, budget accounting all touched the same hot path). The fixer is the right pattern when fixes interact; per-finding fixers would have stepped on each other.
- **Review findings, all applied:**
  - **P0 correctness C-01:** Parallel worker didn't drop biased-partial IS windows (when `is_timed_out=True AND 0 < len(is_combos) < total_combos_per_window`, serial path drops the window because the winner came from a deterministic combo prefix; parallel just kept it). Fixed by adding `biased_partial: bool` field to `WindowOutput`; main route skips windows with the flag set AND propagates `timed_out=True`. Serial path mirrored — `break` replaced with `continue` for behavior parity (parallel can't break out of already-submitted workers; serial follows suit so both paths return identical structure).
  - **P0 reliability R-1:** `with ProcessPoolExecutor(...) as ex:` block was blocking on `__exit__` for the full duration of every running worker, defeating the `shutdown(wait=False, cancel_futures=True)` call. Replaced with explicit `ex = ProcessPoolExecutor(...); try: ... finally: ex.shutdown(wait=False, cancel_futures=True)` so the main thread returns immediately after timeout; spawned subprocesses are cancelled-if-pending or left to complete naturally with their own deadline.
  - **P0 reliability R-2 / P1 correctness C-03:** `_init_worker` failure was silently dropping windows with `timed_out=False` because `BrokenProcessPool` was caught by the generic `except Exception` and logged as a warning. Now caught separately and re-raised as `HTTPException(500, "WFA worker pool crashed")` so operators see a real error.
  - **P1 correctness C-02:** Workers were each given the full `timeout_secs` (e.g., 120s), so a stuck worker could keep running for 120s after the outer `wait()` returned. Switched to shared `deadline = monotonic() + timeout_secs` computed once at dispatch; each submission gets `max(0, deadline - monotonic())` — workers see a shrinking budget as the deadline approaches and exit early when it's exhausted.
  - **P1 correctness C-04 / kieran KP-04 (converged):** `_wfa_start = monotonic()` was being captured AFTER `_fetch`, so `remaining_budget = _WFA_TIMEOUT_SECS - (monotonic() - _wfa_start)` was always ≈ `_WFA_TIMEOUT_SECS`. The full fetch + window-construction time was effectively "free" w.r.t. the budget, allowing wall-clock to silently exceed the documented 120s cap. Hoisted `_wfa_start` to the top of `run_walk_forward`.
  - **P1 reliability R-6:** Worker `HTTPException(500)` from inner `run_backtest` was silently swallowed. Now caught separately before the generic `except Exception` and re-raised.
  - **P1 kieran KP-02:** `_make_dropped_window` was dead code with a self-contradicting docstring (`"Not inserted into results in the serial path — break handles it"`). Deleted.
  - **P2 kieran KP-06:** `_MIN_WINDOWS_FOR_POOL = 4` was a bare magic constant; added a comment block citing the observed spawn cost (~100ms/worker × 8 ≈ 800ms break-even).
  - **P2 correctness C-05:** Test comment in `test_timeout_drops_biased_window` updated to describe the new mechanism (negative `remaining_budget` triggers `timeout_secs <= 0` early-return in `_run_windows_serial`).
- **Result:** NVDA 5m 1y WFA — 57s real-world → ~18s (~3.2× via 8-core parallel); in-process 36s F169 → **14.97s** F166 (~2.4×). Combined F162+F163+F169+F166 win on the same workload: 1m55s → ~15s = **~7.7× cumulative**. 425/425 pytest green. New follow-up TODOs: F172 (direct unit tests for the parallel path — currently 100% gated by `_FORCE_SERIAL=True` test seam); F173 (cleanup pass — type annotations, NamedTuple for worker args, extract shared `_run_window_core` to eliminate the worker/serial duplication that caused the C-01 P0); F174 (audit module-level side effects re-executed by every spawned worker — Alpaca SDK already runs 8× per WFA, future additions could 8× silently).

### F169 — Pre-slice df in WFA (interactive, Tier B)

- After F162+F163 shipped, user ran a real WFA on NVDA 5m 1y (39 windows × 10 IS combos + 39 OOS, screenshot 18:15) and reported 1m55s wall-clock — much worse than the ~3s our daily-grid profile suggested. Per-backtest cost was ~270ms instead of the ~10ms we'd measured on daily. Two root causes: (a) per-window `run_backtest` re-`_fetch`'d a different `(start, end)` cache-key combination, missing the in-process TTL cache and round-tripping to Alpaca 78× per WFA run; (b) the per-bar Python eval_rule loop scales linearly with bar count, and 5m has ~50× more bars than daily. Discussion → ship A (pre-slice df) now and B (window-level parallel = F166) next. C (vectorize eval_rule = F170) deferred — touches the most-called function in the system.
- **[F169](TODO-archive.md#f169)** New optional kwarg `run_backtest(req, *, df: pd.DataFrame | None = None, ...)` and `run_grid(base, params, timeout_secs, *, df=None)` short-circuit `_fetch` when a df is provided. WFA computes `is_df = full_df.iloc[is_s:is_e+1]` and `oos_df = full_df.iloc[oos_s:oos_e+1]` per window and passes them through `run_grid` (IS) and `run_backtest` (OOS). Optimizer NOT wired — single ticker/date-range per grid, in-process TTL hits cleanly. F169 is also a prerequisite for F166's worker design (workers will accept a pre-sliced df via initializer to skip cold-fetch dominance of spawn cost).
- **Orchestrator workflow (Tier B):** Opus orchestrator + Sonnet implementer → orchestrator verify (424/424 pytest + real WFA timing on user's exact config: 1m55s → 44.82s) → parallel 2-persona review (correctness + kieran-python) → synthesize → mechanical fixes applied directly.
- **Review findings, applied directly:**
  - **P1 kieran KP-1:** Drop the string-quoted `df: "pd.DataFrame | None"` annotations — `pd` is already imported in both files, no forward-reference need. Switched to `df: pd.DataFrame | None = None`.
  - **P1 kieran KP-2:** `df` was positional-or-keyword in `run_grid`; made it kwarg-only behind `*,` for parity with `run_backtest`.
  - **P2 kieran KP-4 + P2 correctness C-02 (converged):** The OOS `run_backtest(oos_req, df=oos_df)` call used the default `include_spy_correlation=True`, so each window did an extra 1d SPY fetch. Passed `include_spy_correlation=False` explicitly — recovered another ~9s on the NVDA 5m WFA (44.82s → **36.05s**, the final number).
  - **P2 kieran KP-3:** One-line comment before the `if df is None` bypass to anchor the rebinding for future readers.
- **C-01 — unintended pre-existing bug fix surfaced by correctness review:** The pre-F169 path used `_fetch(end=is_end_date)`, which inherits yfinance/Alpaca exclusive-end semantics, so actual per-window bar counts were `is_bars - 1` and `oos_bars - 1`. F169's `df.iloc[is_s:is_e+1]` produces exactly `req.is_bars` and `req.oos_bars` rows, matching the documented window semantics. Pre-existing WFA results with the same config will shift slightly. Not a regression; a silent fix. New fence test `test_f169_df_slice_size_matches_window_config` captures every `df=` kwarg passed through the WFA loop and asserts each length is exactly `is_bars` or `oos_bars` — would catch any future regression to the off-by-one.
- **Result:** NVDA 5m 1y WFA 1m55s → **36.05s** (~3.2× faster), measured against the user's exact reported config. 425/425 pytest green (one new fence test). F166 (window-level parallel) is now unblocked.

### F163 — Shared run_grid helper, commit 2 of 2 (interactive, Tier B → shipped serial-only)

- F163 was originally scoped as "ProcessPoolExecutor over the grid" — give 4–8× wall-clock on top of F162. Sonnet implementer built the parallel path. Profile result was the surprise of the day: **serial-with-shared-cache (0.25s for 24 combos) beat parallel-with-8-workers (2.29s for the same grid)**. F162's `indicator_cache` lifecycle, when extended across all combos in a single grid call, amortizes MACD/RSI/ATR/MA/BB compute to ~zero — per-backtest cost drops to ~10ms. ProcessPool spawn-context startup (~100ms/worker on macOS) + cold yfinance fetch per worker + per-worker cache rebuild are total dead weight against that. Larger grid (72 combos) was 2.35s parallel — also worse. The parallel design that *would* win is window-level parallelism for WFA with a persistent pool shipping the df via initializer; deferred as F166.
- **[F163](TODO-archive.md#f163)** Shipped scope: `backend/routes/grid_runner.py` with a single `run_grid(base, params, timeout_secs) -> (results, timed_out, skipped)`. Replaces the copy-pasted `for combo in product(...): run_backtest()` loops in `routes/backtest_optimizer.py:88-122` (one cache for the whole grid) and `routes/walk_forward.py:309-327` IS-only (one cache per window; OOS stays serial via direct `wf_mod.run_backtest`). Upfront `_apply_param` validation surfaces 400s immediately rather than per-combo. 4xx → skip+counter, 5xx → re-raise unchanged from the prior semantics. Test seam: `grid_runner.run_backtest` is a module-level binding so monkeypatches retarget there; WFA tests dual-patch (`grid_runner_mod` for IS + `wf_mod` for OOS = same mock function, call-count dispatch preserved across the seam split).
- **Orchestrator workflow (Tier C → Tier B):** Opus orchestrator + Sonnet implementer (parallel path) → orchestrator verify → **profile showed parallel slower than serial** → user redirected to "ship serial-only, add C-design as TODO" → orchestrator stripped the ProcessPool code + `_FORCE_SERIAL` flag, repointed tests, re-ran. Originally planned 5-persona Tier C review; downgraded to Tier B (2 personas: correctness + kieran-python) after scope shrunk to ~110 lines + no concurrency.
- **Test seam migration:** 8 patches in `tests/test_backtest_optimizer.py` retargeted (`opt_mod` → `grid_runner_mod`). 17 patches in `tests/test_walk_forward.py` retargeted AND mirrored — every IS patch is paired with a sibling `wf_mod.run_backtest` patch so the OOS call uses the same mock. `test_timeout_drops_biased_window` rewritten to mock both `wf_mod.monotonic` (per-window budget check) and `grid_runner_mod.monotonic` (per-combo budget check) — the timeout now fires inside the helper.
- **P1 caught in review:** both reviewers converged on `OptimizeResponse.skipped = total_combos - len(results)` silently conflating timed-out-not-reached combos with genuine 4xx/exception drops. Fixed by promoting `run_grid` to return a 3-tuple `(results, timed_out, skipped)` with an explicit counter — restores the old semantic. Two dead `grid_runner_mod` imports in validation tests removed. Mechanical fixes applied directly.
- **Result:** 24-combo SPY daily grid 1.53s (F162) → **0.25s** (F163) = ~6× via cache lifecycle alone. 424/424 pytest green. New TODOs: F166 (window-level parallel WFA + persistent pool), F167 (test helper for the dual-patch boilerplate), F168 (direct `run_grid` unit tests). Combined F162+F163 win on the same 24-combo grid: 2.59s → 0.25s = **10× faster**.

### F162 — Backtest grid hot-path optimization, commit 1 of 2 (interactive, Tier B)

- User asked to profile WFA + optimizer for speed wins and share infra between them. cProfile on a representative 24-combo SPY-daily grid: 2.59s / 108ms per backtest. Hotspots: per-bar `pandas __getitem__` (34%), `_compute_spy_correlation` running every call (23%), `_format_time` per-bar (9%), `compute_indicators` recomputed across combos (5%).
- **[F162](TODO-archive.md#f162)** Four landed changes, all reachable via the existing `run_backtest` import — no concurrency complexity, both call sites benefit: (1) `run_backtest(req, *, include_spy_correlation=True, indicator_cache=None)` kwargs, with `@router.post("/api/backtest")` split into a `backtest_endpoint` wrapper so FastAPI doesn't body-wrap the new params; (2) vectorized `shared._format_time_index` using `as_unit('s').view('int64')` — the kieran-python + correctness reviewers both independently caught that the naive `// 1_000_000_000` divisor was wrong (pandas 2.0+ preserves source resolution: yfinance `[s]`, Alpaca `[us]`) and converged on `as_unit('s')` as the durable fix that doesn't hardcode a resolution; (3) `compute_indicators(..., cache=indicator_cache)` with new `_get_or_compute(cache, key, factory)` helper deduping MACD/RSI/ATR/MA/BB across grid combos; (4) numpy array pre-extraction (`close_arr`, `date_strs`) before the bar loop, with the 4 in-loop sites switching to array indexing — `eval_rule` and the position state machine left untouched on purpose. Wired through `routes/backtest_optimizer.py` (per-grid cache) + `routes/walk_forward.py` IS loop only (per-window cache; OOS unchanged so it still gets full SPY-corr).
- **Orchestrator workflow (Tier B):** Opus orchestrator + Sonnet implementer → orchestrator verify (424/424 pytest after route-handler split fix for `test_backtest_short`) → parallel 2-persona review (correctness + kieran-python) → orchestrator synthesize → mechanical fixes applied directly. The implementer hit a `mock_run_backtest(req)` brittleness across 25+ test sites and stopped as instructed; orchestrator added `**kwargs` to all of them as a mechanical compat fix.
- **P0 caught in review:** vectorized timestamp output was off by 1000× under pandas 3.0.2 (every intraday bar in equity_curve, baseline_curve, rule_signals, regime_series would have shipped corrupted unix timestamps). Both reviewers found it independently; the divisor-based fix kieran suggested was correct for the current `[us]` storage, but the resolution-agnostic `as_unit('s')` is what landed since it survives a future pandas resolution change. New `tests/test_shared_format_time.py` round-trips `_format_time_index` against the scalar `_format_time` across `[s, ms, us, ns]` plus tz-naive + daily + empty — 7 tests that would have failed loud on this bug.
- **P1 fixes also applied:** `list[int] | list[str]` return type on `_format_time_index`; `dict[tuple, object]` annotation on both `indicator_cache` locals; `_get_or_compute` helper extracted (collapsed 5 copy-pasted if/cache-hit/else/cache-store blocks across MACD/RSI/ATR-14/MA/BB/ATR-variable into uniform 3-line calls); lambda-mock at `test_walk_forward.py:325` fixed (correctness found this one — distinct from the `**kwargs` pattern). Refactor squeezed another ~6% on top.
- **Result:** 24-combo grid 2.59s → 1.53s = **41% faster** (108ms → 64ms per backtest). 424/424 pytest green. New TODOs surfaced: F163 (shared `run_grid` helper + ProcessPool, commit 2 of 2 — gives 4–8× on top of F162), F164 (tighten test mocks to explicit signatures), F165 (runtime guard for cache contract via `id(close)` in keys).

### C28 — Walk-forward analysis (interactive, Tier C, branch `feat/c28-walk-forward-analysis`)

- User asked for walk-forward implementation: validate strategies on rolling/anchored IS-OOS windows before deploying capital, replace the ad-hoc "tweak params, re-run on same dates" workflow that overfits silently. Direct extension of C22 auto-optimizer; built on top of `_apply_param` and `_VALID_METRICS`/`_MAX_COMBOS` from the existing optimizer route.
- **[C28](TODO-archive.md#c28)** Backend (`backend/routes/walk_forward.py`, 463 lines): `POST /api/backtest/walk_forward`. Pydantic `WalkForwardRequest` with `is_bars`/`oos_bars`/`gap_bars`/`step_bars`/`expand_train`/`metric`/`min_trades_is` and a list of `WalkForwardParam` (grid). Returns per-window `WindowResult` (IS-best params, IS+OOS metrics, stability_tag), rescaled `stitched_equity`, `wfe` (mean-of-Sharpes form), `param_cv`, `low_windows_warn`, `timed_out`. **Capital-reset sawtooth fix:** each OOS curve is rescaled by `prev_final_equity / base.initial_capital` so the stitched curve compounds correctly. **Stability tags (5):** `stable_plateau` / `spike` / `low_trades_is` / `no_oos_trades` / `no_is_trades`, typed as `Literal`. Neighborhood-stability detection: IS winner is `stable_plateau` if ≥60% of its grid neighbors are in the top-quartile of IS Sharpe; else `spike`. Regime is unconditionally stripped from base requests in v1; intraday intervals rejected with 400 (sub-day window boundaries deferred). Frontend (`WalkForwardPanel.tsx`, 606 lines) is a self-contained sub-tab in `Results.tsx` alongside `OptimizerPanel` with its own lightweight-charts instance for the stitched equity curve.
- **Plan-first workflow:** ~600-line plan at `docs/plans/2026-05-12-walk-forward-analysis.md` was iterated through 3 parallel doc-review personas (coherence, feasibility, scope-guardian) BEFORE implementation. Scope review cut `disable_regime` field, `oos_equity_rescaled` per-window field, two configurability constants, and intraday support (deferred); kept neighborhood-stability detection over reviewer objection because per-window granularity is the v1 anti-overfit value-add beyond WFE itself.
- **Orchestrator workflow (Tier C):** Opus orchestrator + parallel Sonnet backend/frontend implementers → orchestrator verify (backend↔frontend contract cross-check: all 12 `WindowResult` + 9 `WalkForwardResponse` fields match) → 6 parallel review personas (correctness, testing, project-standards, kieran-python, kieran-typescript, adversarial-opus) → orchestrator synthesize → mechanical fixes applied directly (Field constraints, `_STABILITY_THRESHOLD` rename, Literal type, `initial_capital > 0` guard, `step_bars >= oos_bars` guard, NaN/Inf rejection, `as any` cast → `UTCTimestamp`, logged-skip instead of bare except) → one Sonnet fixer for the holistic algorithmic + test-rewrite work.
- **P0 fix — anchored mode was completely broken:** the original window-computation block produced **identical `(is_start, is_end, oos_start, oos_end)` on every iteration** when `expand_train=True` because `is_end_idx = is_start_idx + req.is_bars - 1` with `is_start_idx=0` is constant. The bug originated in the plan's pseudocode (orchestrator) and the implementer faithfully reproduced it. Caught by the correctness reviewer; also independently flagged by adversarial. Test `test_anchored_vs_rolling_window_counts` only asserted `is_start` is constant (true in both broken and fixed code) — didn't catch it. Fixer rewrote the math so anchored mode: `oos_start_idx = oos_end_idx - oos_bars + 1`; `is_end_idx = oos_start_idx - 1 - gap_bars`; `is_start_idx = 0`. IS window now expands monotonically as OOS slides forward. Test strengthened to assert `len(set(is_end)) > 1`, monotone sort, distinct `oos_start` per window, `len(r_windows) == len(a_windows)`.
- **P0 fix — negative `gap_bars` data leakage:** no validation; `gap_bars=-50, is_bars=100, oos_bars=100` made IS=[0,99] and OOS=[50,149] overlap by 50 bars, silently inflating WFE. Fixed mechanically via `gap_bars: int = Field(0, ge=0)`. Also added `params` `min_length=1`/`max_length=_MAX_PARAMS`, `values` `min_length=1`/`max_length=10` + `@field_validator` rejecting NaN/Inf, `is_bars`/`oos_bars` `gt=0`, `initial_capital > 0`, `step_bars < oos_bars` rejection.
- **P1 fix — timeout-mid-grid bias:** when `_WFA_TIMEOUT_SECS` fired mid-IS-grid, the window was still appended with a biased winner picked from a deterministic prefix of the combo product. Now: `if timed_out and len(is_combos) < total_combos_per_window: break` — drop the window entirely, never report partial-grid results.
- **Tests:** backend 18 → 39 tests (`TestPureHelpers` class for `_combo_key` / `_get_neighbor_keys` / `_deduplicate_by_time` edge cases; new tests for `low_trades_is`, no-neighbors-1value-grid, `wfe=None` branches, scale_factor preservation across no_oos windows, gap_bars positive, timeout drops biased window, negative-gap rejection, empty/too-many/NaN values rejection, zero-initial-capital, overlapping-step rejection). Frontend 4 → 8 tests (`low_windows_warn` callout, `timed_out` callout, all-5-stability-tags exhaustiveness, WFE badge color thresholds). Strengthened 6 existing tests with deterministic call-counter dispatch and tight tolerances instead of vacuous `>= 0` assertions.
- **Verification:** orchestrator confirmed 39/39 WFA tests pass, 416/416 backend suite, `npm run build` clean, 8/8 frontend WFA tests. **Not visually verified** in-session — flagged for next dev-server boot.
- **Three more iteration cycles after the UX one** (all in-branch, no separate PRs):
  - **Provider-aware pre-flight estimate.** First UX pass calibrated against yfinance's intraday day-limits; user was on Alpaca which has full intraday history. Estimate said "~4 windows / ~2s", actual was 63 windows / 2 minutes. Renamed `INTERVAL_MAX_DAYS` → `YAHOO_INTRADAY_MAX_DAYS`, gated the clamp behind `(lastRequest.source ?? 'yahoo') === 'yahoo'`. Per-backtest cost was also a flat 50ms — too optimistic for large windows; changed to `max(0.05, isBars * 2.2e-4)` seconds. Calibrated against the user's observed 2-min run for 5m/IS=1560/63-windows.
  - **Spike-paradox interpretation branch.** User ran AMD 5m WFA with IS=4000, got WFE=1.776 (above the 0.7 healthy threshold) but every one of 6 windows was tagged `Spike`. The "Healthy walk-forward" green callout fired correctly per threshold but masked a real concern: one outlier OOS window (+4.32%) was pulling WFE up despite each IS winner sitting at an isolated peak. Added a new amber callout branch (between `wfe===null` and `wfe>=0.7`) that fires when WFE is high but every window is spike, telling the user to widen the param grid and extend the date range.
  - **Full-width pane + sub-tab persistence.** Panel root had `maxWidth: 960` capping the table and chart regardless of pane size — removed in favor of `width: 100%`. Sub-tabs (sensitivity, optimizer, walk_forward) were using conditional `&&` rendering in `Results.tsx`, which unmounted on tab switch and lost expensive run results. Switched all three to the App.tsx-style `display: 'none'` pattern (F152 precedent in CLAUDE.md). 30s+ runs no longer disappear on a tab click.
  - **Per-strategy config persistence.** Walk-Forward and Optimizer input configs now persist to localStorage keyed by `(ticker, interval, source)`. Survives tab switches AND page reloads. Result objects are NOT persisted (MB-scale). Param paths validated against current `paramOptions` on restore so a strategy rule edit doesn't restore a now-invalid reference. Replaces the prior "reset on lastRequest change" hard-wipe with a load-then-fall-back-to-defaults flow.
- **UX iteration after first user run (post-merge polish):** user ran WFA on 5m intraday with daily-suitable defaults (IS=252, OOS=63 → 3 trading days IS / 0.8 days OOS), got 103 windows all flagged "below IS trade minimum", hit 120s timeout, couldn't tell the result was noise. Five fixes in `WalkForwardPanel.tsx`: (1) Interval-aware default IS/OOS map keyed off `lastRequest.interval` — 5m gets 1560/390, 1m gets 780/195 (yfinance 7-day cap), 1d keeps Pardo 252/63. Re-fires when interval changes. (2) Pre-flight cost estimate (`~N windows × M combos = Z backtests, ~T seconds`) — colors amber when >2× timeout, red when <2 windows fit OR `step_bars < oos_bars`. (3) Results table horizontal scroll + short param labels (`buy_rule_0_params_period` → `buy0.period`; full path on hover). (4) Interpretation callout under WFE badge with 6 prioritized branches — low-trades-IS wins over WFE branches because it explains the bad WFE. (5) Verdict column moved to position 2 with short labels (`Plateau` / `Spike` / `Thin IS` / `No OOS` / `Failed`). Tier B review caught: nWindows formula was missing `oos_bars + gap_bars + 1` (overstated window count, diverged when `step ≠ oos`); `shortParamLabel` collided when two paths shared first+last segment (now includes numeric rule-index → `buy0.period`); `catch (_)` would break CI lint (changed to bare `catch`). Hoisted `defaultBars` to module scope, lazy useState initializers. 2 new tests pin the formula + label disambiguation. 13 → 15 tests.
- **Intraday support added same-branch (post-review):** the v1 plan deferred intraday with a "queue a follow-up C-item" disposition, but the fix turned out to be ~30 lines so it landed on the same branch. (a) New `_format_boundary(ts, interval)` helper in `walk_forward.py` uses `"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S"` for intraday intervals so adjacent IS-end and OOS-start bars on the same calendar day don't collide on string equality (which would otherwise re-fetch the full day and leak IS into OOS via the provider). (b) `YahooProvider.fetch()` in `shared.py` loosened from `datetime.strptime('%Y-%m-%d', ...)` strict to `pd.Timestamp(...)` which accepts both date and datetime strings; passes `datetime` objects to `yf.Ticker.history()` to avoid yfinance's string-parsing quirks. AlpacaProvider already handled both via `pd.Timestamp(end, tz='UTC')`. (c) Removed the `if req.base.interval in _INTRADAY_INTERVALS: raise 400` guard. (d) Provider day-limit (`_INTERVAL_MAX_DAYS`: 7 days for 1m, 60 days for 5m/15m/30m, 730 days for 60m/1h) now surfaces in the "not enough bars" error message so the user knows why their wide-range request didn't return enough data. 2 new tests (intraday datetime-precision boundaries, intraday provider-limit error message); old `test_intraday_interval_rejected` removed. WFA tests 39 → 40, backend suite 416 → 417.
- **Follow-ups already in TODO** (queued at plan time, not surfaced after-the-fact): **[C29]** cluster-centroid IS selector (upgrade path from neighborhood-stability tag once spike-tag rate justifies the k-means complexity), **[C30]** Combinatorial Purged Cross-Validation (PBO score, distributional OOS evidence), **[C31]** calendar-term window inputs (skfolio-style `"3MS"` strings).

### F155 — Regime-mode silent-fallback closed (interactive, Tier B)

- User reported (with screenshot): trade tooltips on an AMD regime-mode strategy showed `stochastic turns_up_below 5.0` even though the visible LONG entry rule was `MA 200 EMA rising over N bars`. F151's localStorage cache fix didn't catch this — the result was fresh from the most recent Run Backtest. Root cause was a different stale-state path: in regime mode the StrategyBuilder hides the single-mode `buyRules` from the UI but the request payload still sends them, and the backend's `b23_mode` required BOTH `long_buy_rules` and `short_buy_rules` to be non-empty before activating the dual-rule path. If either side was empty (very common for users only configuring one direction), b23_mode flipped off and the backtester silently ran the hidden unified `buy_rules` — typically still holding rules from before regime was enabled. User picked option #1 (strictest): once regime is on, the unified rules never run.
- **[F155](TODO-archive.md#f155)** Backend (`backend/routes/backtest.py`): `b23_mode = bool(req.regime and req.regime.enabled)` — loosened from the previous "both sides non-empty" gate. Migration block uses `or []` uniformly across all four direction-specific rule lists so None inputs don't crash. Two symmetric display fixes: `display_buy_rules = active_buy if b23_mode else buy_rules` for the entry trade record + entry signal_trace; `display_sell_rules = active_sell if b23_mode and position_direction is not None else sell_rules` for the exit trade record + in-position signal_trace. Out-of-position signal_trace (the "MISSED (no position)" branch) suppressed in b23_mode entirely — there's no `position_direction` to disambiguate, and showing the hidden unified `sell_rules` in debug output would leak the rules the user thought they removed. Frontend (`StrategyBuilder.tsx`): payload drops the `.some(r => r.indicator)` guard at line 371 (always sends `long_*` + `short_*` rule fields when regime is enabled); small italic hint added to the LONG and SHORT entry-rule panels saying "No long/short entry rules — no long/short positions will open. Exits/stops still run on existing positions." when that side is empty.
- **Orchestrator workflow (Tier B):** Opus orchestrator + two parallel Sonnet implementers (backend `backtest.py` + tests; frontend `StrategyBuilder.tsx`) → orchestrator verify → 2 parallel Sonnet personas (correctness + kieran-python) → orchestrator synthesize → one Sonnet fixer for the holistic P1 + P2 (correctness P1: exit-side asymmetry — `_fired_rules(sell_rules, ...)` at line 728 and 3 sell-side signal_trace sites still used the raw unified `sell_rules`. Kieran-python P2: regression tests couldn't distinguish the unified rule label from the direction-specific one because both used `condition="above", value=0`). Tier B per F136 — `[medium]` regime-semantics change. ~50 lines code + ~250 lines tests across 3 files.
- **Tests:** 5 new tests in `test_backtest_short.py`: long-only fires long not unified; short-only fires short not unified; both-empty produces no trades; regime-off regression pin (unified still drives entries in single mode); exit-side display test (direction-specific sell_rules show in exit trade record, not unified). Distinguishable rule conditions ("above" vs "below") so a future revert of `display_buy_rules` / `display_sell_rules` would fail the assertions. 12/12 pass.
- **Verification:** orchestrator read both implementer diffs and the fixer diff against spec; pytest `test_backtest_short.py` 12/12; `npm run build` 275ms clean; AST parse on backtest.py clean. **Not visually verified** in-session — flagged for next dev-server boot.
- **Deferred review findings:**
  - correctness C2 (P2, test-coverage gap for signal_trace b23 path with `debug=True`) — addressed in part by the new exit-side test, but a dedicated `debug=True` trace test would be additive. Filed as **[F156]** below.
  - correctness C3 (P3, scoping doc for `active_buy` at the `display_buy_rules` site) — defensible to leave; the scoping is correct and the comment would be narrating mechanics. No action.
  - kieran-python KP-1 (P2, comment on line 328 about `all_rules` accumulation imprecise — frames it as a b23-only concern but the unified rules are already in `all_rules` from earlier in the function) — minor comment polish. Filed as **[F157]** below.
- **Follow-ups surfaced (2 new):**
  - **[F156]** Add a `debug=True` signal_trace test for b23_mode that asserts the trace output uses direction-specific rules and that the "MISSED (no position)" trace is suppressed when flat in b23_mode. Closes correctness C2's residual gap. [easy] [testing]
  - **[F157]** Tighten the comment at `backend/routes/backtest.py:328` about `all_rules` extension. Today says "Combine all rules so indicators are computed for every rule across both sets" — should clarify that the unified `buy_rules` + `sell_rules` were already added to `all_rules` at line 314, and the b23 branch appends direction-specific rules on top. Pure prose polish. [easy] [polish]

### F151 + F152 — Backtester stale-state bug pair (interactive, Tier A)

- User-reported "caching bug on the backtester: keeps running the same rules for buys even when muted or deleted." Systematic debugging confirmed the backend is clean (`eval_rules` correctly skips `r.muted`; live curl with both sides muted returns `num_trades: 0`) and traced the symptom to two distinct frontend stale-state caches that match the report.
- **[F151](TODO-archive.md#f151)** `frontend/src/App.tsx` `_cachedBacktest` IIFE restored the previous backtest result on page mount when only ticker/start/end/interval matched — rule mutations were ignored. After muting/deleting a rule and refreshing the tab, the chart still showed trades fired by the now-muted rule. New `loadStrategyCache()` reads `'strategylab-strategy'`; the restore predicate now compares 12 rule/logic field pairs (camelCase ↔ snake_case) plus `regime` via `JSON.stringify` and returns `null` on any mismatch.
- **[F152](TODO-archive.md#f152)** `frontend/src/features/discovery/SignalScanner.tsx:29` captured `loadStrategies()` once at mount and never refreshed. Since tabs use `display: 'none'` rather than unmount (`App.tsx:198/369/372`), Discovery kept running pre-edit rule snapshots after StrategyBuilder edits. Mirrored the existing `AddBotBar.tsx:140` `onFocus={loadStrategies}` pattern at the window level: new `useEffect` registers `focus` + `storage` listeners with cleanup, and `setStrategies` is now exposed from the `useState` destructure.
- **Orchestrator workflow:** Opus orchestrator + two parallel Sonnet implementers (files independent: `App.tsx` ↔ `SignalScanner.tsx`, zero overlap). Tier A per F136 (aggregate bundle ~30 lines added across 2 files, both `[easy] [hardening]`, no contract-surface change). No persona review per Tier A rules.
- **Verification:** orchestrator read both diffs against spec (`App.tsx:41-87` + `SignalScanner.tsx:29 / 51-59`). `cd frontend && npm run build` clean (✓ built in 278ms, only the pre-existing chunk-size warning). Bug A reproduction path: backtest with rule A → mute A → reload → `_cachedBacktest` now returns `null` (rule signature differs from cached request) → chart and Results re-mount with no stale trades. Bug B reproduction path: open Discovery tab → switch to Chart tab → edit a saved strategy → switch back to Discovery → window `focus` event refreshes `strategies` from localStorage.
- **Visual verification:** not visually verified in-session (no live browser). Both fixes are pure state-management changes; the restore predicate either returns the cache or `null` (existing rendering paths handle both) and the SignalScanner change adds a no-op listener for the "no edits between focus events" case. Flagged for next time the dev server is running.
- **Follow-ups surfaced (2 new):**
  - **[F153]** Long-term, the `_cachedBacktest` predicate's growing field-pair list will drift if a new request field is added without updating the comparator. A central `requestSignature(req)` helper in `shared/types` that both the cache writer and restore predicate consume would prevent the drift. Defer as `[easy] [arch]` — not urgent until the next backtest field is added.
  - **[F154]** Other consumers of `'strategylab-saved-strategies'` should be audited for the same "snapshot at mount, never refresh" pattern that F152 fixed in SignalScanner. Candidates: `BotCard.tsx:357` reads `s.buyRules` from a strategy reference, `StrategyComparison.tsx:5` uses `loadSavedStrategies` similarly. AddBotBar already handles it via `onFocus`. Defer as `[easy] [hardening]`.

### F139 — chronic event-loop contamination closed (fully clean suite for the first time)

- Single-item orchestrator pass: haiku diagnose → sonnet implement → Tier B review (correctness + kieran-python in parallel) → orchestrator-applied convergent fix → commit. ~7 min wall-clock.
- **[F139](TODO.md#f139)** Root cause (per haiku diagnostic): prior tests calling `asyncio.run()` (test_bot_runner.py, test_body_size_middleware.py, etc.) close their internal loop and leave the main thread with no default. `test_ibkr_provider_submit_market_order` then triggers `broker.py:570` lazy-import of `ib_insync`, which pulls in `eventkit/util.py:24` calling `asyncio.get_event_loop()` at module init — RuntimeError on Python 3.10+ (3.12 here). The chronic F139 + F148 pair has shown up on every overnight build for 6 consecutive runs.
- **Fix:** new `backend/tests/conftest.py` with a single autouse fixture `_f139_ensure_event_loop` that creates + sets a fresh loop per test, then `loop.close() + asyncio.set_event_loop(None)` on teardown. Compatible with pytest-asyncio 1.3.0 (`asyncio_mode=auto`) because pytest-asyncio uses `event_loop_policy`, not the thread default.
- **Morning review (correctness + kieran-python, both Sonnet) converged on one P1 ~0.72:** the original draft left the closed loop visible on the thread default ("don't set to None" — supposed to help pytest-asyncio). Reviewers correctly observed this was unverified against pytest-asyncio internals and left stale state. Applied the convergent fix: `set_event_loop(None)` on teardown. Inline comment in conftest.py now documents the morning-review correction. Other reviewer findings deferred: kieran's `_f139_` prefix nit (kept for grep traceability), correctness P1-F2 leaked loop in `_make_ibkr_provider` test helper (filed as [F150](TODO-archive.md#f150) — pre-existing, not introduced by F139).
- **Result: full backend pytest 372 / 0** — first fully-clean run since the F120/F40/F46/F107/F108/F134 sweep began. Both chronic failures (F148 yesterday + F139 tonight) are gone. The overnight builder pipeline should report a clean suite tomorrow morning.

### F95 + F100 — SymbolField rollout + BotManager.load() migration (Tier C, full 6-persona panel)

- First Tier C item of the day. The persistence-schema-change override per F136 promoted this from `[medium]`'s normal Tier B (2 personas) up to the full 4-6 panel. Full workflow: 1 haiku explore + 1 sonnet implementer + **6 parallel sonnet reviewers** (correctness, security, maintainability, testing, project-standards, kieran-python) + 1 sonnet fixer + Opus orchestrator. 8 dispatched agents on one item.
- **[F95](TODO.md#f95)** applies `SymbolField` (via existing `Annotated[str, BeforeValidator(normalize_symbol)]` from models.py) to 4 remaining sites: `StrategyRequest.ticker`, `BotConfig.symbol`, `ScanRequest.symbols: list[SymbolField]`, `PerformanceRequest.symbol`. ScanRequest also got F93's dedup pattern via a new `@field_validator`.
- **[F100](TODO.md#f100)** adds `normalize_symbol(raw_symbol)` pre-pass + a named-control-flow try-split in `BotManager.load()`: one try for symbol normalize + migrate_rule (catches `ValueError`); separate try for `BotConfig(**cfg_dict)` (catches `pydantic.ValidationError`); broad `except Exception` fallback. Each emits a distinct WARNING with `%r` for bot_id (newline-safe).
- **[F43](TODO.md#f43) and [F82](TODO.md#f82) closed** as side-effects. F43's log-injection vector audit confirms every `_log_trade()` call site now reads SymbolField-validated input. F82's `ScanRequest.symbols` cap is satisfied by SymbolField's 20-char per-element regex + the new dedup validator.
- **6-persona review delivered substantial cross-converged signal that the orchestrator alone would have missed:**
  - **COR-001 P1 (0.92):** `migrate_rule` loop was OUTSIDE the inner try block. A malformed `Rule` entry would have aborted ALL remaining bots — exactly the silent-data-loss class F100 was filed to prevent. Caught only by reading the per-bot loop carefully.
  - **K1 + COR-002 P1/P2 (0.90):** The original `except ValueError` mislabeled BotConfig construction failures as "invalid symbol". Fix: split into normalize-then-construct named-control-flow with explicit `ValidationError` import.
  - **SEC-01 P2 (0.82):** bot_id was logged via `%s`. Crafted `bots.json` entry with newline in bot_id would have injected fake log lines.
  - **COR-003 P2 (0.95):** ScanRequest had no dedup — `["AAPL", "aapl", "  aapl  "]` would have triggered 3 scans on the same symbol.
  - **M2 P2 (0.90):** 4 dead `.upper()` calls on SymbolField-typed values (`config.symbol.upper()`, `req.symbol.upper()`) — removed for contract clarity.
  - **PS-01/02/05 P2:** Banner / inline `# F100:` comments violated CLAUDE.md no-comments rule. Removed.
- **Verification:** 363/364 pass (F139 still the lone pre-existing). 13 new tests added across `test_models.py`, `test_trading.py`, `test_bot_state.py` (including missing-symbol-key, dedup, HTTP-level normalization, broad-except path).

### F129 + F130 — signal_engine DoS hardening (Tier B, full orchestrator)

- Second Tier B item of the day. Combined two related signal-engine DoS items into one orchestrator cycle (same file, related theme). Full pipeline: haiku explore → sonnet implementer → 2 parallel sonnet reviewers (correctness + kieran-python) → 1 sonnet fixer → Opus orchestrator.
- **Explore phase (haiku):** identified that `Rule.value` is used as BOTH a lookback bars count AND a comparison threshold (e.g., `price > 100000`). This ruled out F129's Option A (`Field(le=500)` on Rule.value), which would break price thresholds on high-priced assets like BRK.A (~$750k). Recommended Option B: clamp at the 3 lookback consumer sites only. For F130, identified all 7 indicator families and recommended a single shared cap (20) for simplicity over the per-family-tuned values originally suggested.
- **Implement phase (sonnet):** extracted `_clamp_lookback(value, default)` and `_INDICATOR_FAMILY_CAP = 20` constant, applied to the 3 lookback consumers + 7 family guards. 10 new tests in `test_signal_engine.py`.
- **Tier B review (correctness + kieran-python in parallel):** cross-converged on a high-confidence P1 — `compute_indicators` raises `ValueError` for cap violations, but `routes/backtest.py` and `routes/backtest_quick.py` outer `except Exception` blocks were converting these into HTTP 500 "backtest failed" with no actionable detail. Correctness P1 (0.95) + kieran K2 (0.85). Additional findings: P2 from correctness on negative `rule.value` propagating to `_clamp_lookback(-5, 10) = -5` (silent pandas OOB); P2 on missing test coverage for 4 of 7 families; kieran K1 (0.90) on quoted forward-ref type annotation; kieran K3 (0.82) recommending `_assert_family_cap(name, specs)` helper extraction over the 7 copy-paste blocks.
- **Fixer phase (sonnet, holistic):** added `except ValueError → 400` handlers in both backtest routes (3 sites total — single-ticker + batch in backtest_quick); floored `_clamp_lookback` to `max(0, min(...))`; unquoted the type annotation (Python 3.14, no `from __future__`); extracted `_assert_family_cap` helper; replaced all 7 inline guards with one-liner calls; added 4 new tests (negative-floor + ATR + ADX + stochastic + volume SMA).
- **[F129](TODO.md#f129)** Final shape: 3 call sites use `_clamp_lookback`, threshold sites stay unclamped. `max(0, ...)` floors negative input. Tests cover {500, 501, 50, None, -5, exact-boundary}.
- **[F130](TODO.md#f130)** Final shape: 7 `_assert_family_cap("NAME", specs)` calls in `compute_indicators`. ValueError now surfaces as 400 in both backtest routes. 11 tests in `test_signal_engine.py` cover the helper + all 7 families + boundary.
- **Verification:** 331/332 pass (F139 still the only pre-existing failure). 37/37 in `test_signal_engine.py`.
- **Tier B validation again:** cross-reviewer convergence caught a real cross-file issue (ValueError → 500) that the implementer didn't see because they only ran `test_signal_engine.py`. Correctness reviewer found it by following the `compute_indicators` import graph into the routes. Validates the persona role at the medium tier.
